# AGENTS.md — Kabmo AI System Instructions & Protocols

## Overview
**Kabmo** serves as the developer web view console and diagnostic workbench for **AI Oh-My-Pi** (`oh-my-pi`), integrated with **remisJS** and **bun** runtime environments.

---

## Runtime: Bun Only

**This application runs exclusively on Bun. Do not introduce Node-only APIs, and do not assume `node <entry>` works.**

- **Server** (`src/server/**`) imports `bun:sqlite` and resolves the `@/` path alias — Node fails on both with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Run it with `bun run src/server/index.ts` (dev, watched) or `NODE_ENV=production bun run src/server/index.ts` (prod). `bun run start` does the latter, and `kabmo serve` is production by default (`--dev` selects the source entry).
- **Tests** run under `bun test`; do not add a Node test runner.
- **A suite that touches the database must isolate it (`isolateDb`/`releaseDb`, `src/test-support/isolated-db.ts`).** The handle is a PROCESS-wide singleton (`globalThis.__kabmoDb`) and `KABMO_DB_PATH` is read only while that handle is being opened, so setting the path inside a test — after another file already opened the handle — reads the developer's real `~/.kabmo/db.sqlite` and WRITES to it. Verified: `bun test src/server/lib/panels/registry.server.test.ts` bumped the real database's mtime and left a test plugin id in its `omp_panel_plugins` row, and the panel scan is what reaches `getDb()` (it reads the disabled set), so a scan-only suite is not exempt. `isolateDb()` closes and clears the cached handle BEFORE pointing the path at a temp file, because the order is the whole point; `releaseDb()` closes it again and drops the directory.
- **CLI** (`src/cli/**`) is Bun too: `#!/usr/bin/env bun` shebang and `@/cli/...` imports, so it needs Bun on `PATH`. It resolves the Bun binary via `resolveBunBin()` and spawns the server with it. **Nothing in the toolchain runs under Node any more** — the client build is `Bun.build` (see Build & Dev Loop).
- **Practical rules:** never swap `bun:sqlite` for a Node driver; never add a `node:` import to `src/client/**` or `src/shared/lib/**`; keep `.ts`/`.tsx` execution in Bun's hands (no `tsx`/`ts-node`).

### Bun-Native APIs vs `node:*` Imports

Bun implements `node:*` builtins natively — they do **not** shell out to a Node binary. Verified: `node:child_process` runs with `node` off `PATH` and `process.execPath` stays `bun`. So a `node:` import is not a correctness bug; it is only a preference when Bun ships a first-class equivalent. **Do not "convert" a `node:` import to a `Bun.*` call when no equivalent exists** — the conversion is either impossible or silently changes behavior.

| Prefer | Instead of | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `Bun.env.X` | `process.env.X` | Same object in Bun (`Bun.env === process.env`), but the Bun name states the runtime. **Server/CLI only** — `Bun.env` does not exist in the browser, so `src/client/**` and `src/shared/**` must never use it. |
| `crypto.randomUUID()` (global) | `import { randomUUID } from 'crypto'` | Global is available; no import needed. |
| `Bun.gzipSync` / `Bun.gunzipSync` | `zlib.gzipSync` / `gunzipSync` | Byte-identical output, verified. |
| `Bun.YAML.parse` / `Bun.YAML.stringify(obj, null, 2)` | `yaml` package | Rust parser, zero-dependency. The third `stringify` argument (space) produces block-style multi-line YAML; without it output is flow-style single-line. **Caveat**: round-trip drops comments — files that must preserve user comments still need a document-model parser (the `yaml` package was removed because no remaining call site needs comment preservation). |
| `Bun.which('bin')` | `which` via `spawnSync` | Direct PATH lookup. |
| `Bun.spawnSync` / `Bun.spawn` | `child_process.exec`/`execFile`/`spawn` | Native `timeout`, `maxBuffer`, `windowsHide`, `detached` (process group), and `signal: AbortSignal` options. Unlike promisified exec, non-zero exits do **not** throw — check `exitCode`; timeout/kill detection uses `signalCode` (Bun sets `killed: true` even for normal non-zero exits). Server-wide: `lib/fs/shell.ts` wraps `sh -c` for shell strings; `lib/omp/rpc/lines.ts` reads NDJSON from Bun `ReadableStream` stdout (replaces `readline`). |
| `Bun.file(p).stream()` | `fs.createReadStream` | Returns a real Web `ReadableStream` for `new Response(...)` — no node-stream adapter or type cast. |
| `Bun.Glob.scanSync({ cwd, onlyFiles: true })` | `readdirSync` + regex filter loops | Rust-native globbing; supports `*.{a,b,c}` alternation. Returns relative paths; size guards still need `statSync`. |
| `await Bun.file(p).text()` / `.json()` | `fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8')` / `fs.promises.readFile` | Only where the call site is already `async`. `Bun.file()` is async; do not force a sync caller to become async just to use it. Also: `.slice(a, b).text()` for partial reads (log follow), `.stat()`, `.exists()`. |
| `await Bun.write(p, data)` | `fs.writeFileSync` / `fs.promises.writeFile` | **`Bun.write` ignores `mode` when *creating* a new file (verified on Bun 1.4.0 and 1.4.2 despite the docs signature)** — it cannot create a `0o600` file; it is honored when overwriting an existing file. Keep `fs.writeFileSync(..., { mode: 0o600 })` for the CLI registry (`src/cli/lib/runtime.js`). |

**These stay `node:*` — Bun has no equivalent, so do not attempt a conversion:**

- **`node:path`** — `Bun.path` is `undefined`. No replacement exists. (The CLI uses its own minimal `src/cli/lib/path-utils.js` for join/resolve/dirname/home instead of importing `node:path`.)
- **`node:fs` directory + metadata ops** — `mkdir`/`readdir`/`stat`/`exists`/`rm`/`rename`/`Dirent`. Bun's own docs point at `node:fs` for directories; `Bun.file()` is for file contents (`stat()`/`exists()` are async BunFile methods where the chain is already async). `Bun.Glob.scanSync` covers readdir+filter scans.
- **`node:fs` sync reads in sync functions** — `Bun.file()` is async; the omp session/config layers are now fully async (2026-09), so sync reads survive only in genuinely sync contexts (module-level path setup, lockfile exclusive-create in `config/mcp.ts`, and the title-slot in-place 256-byte `r+` write where an offset write is required — Bun's FileSink cannot seek). Convert a read only when the enclosing call chain is already async; CLI bootstrap stays sync deliberately: sequential dependencies (mkdir → open fd → spawn → write registry), no concurrency to exploit, and signal-handler paths must not depend on pending promises.
- **`node:os`** — `Bun.os` is `undefined`. **Never replace `os.homedir()`/`os.tmpdir()` with `Bun.env.HOME`/`TMPDIR`/`TEMP` anywhere** — the env vars are absent when a process runs without a shell (cron, launchd, systemd, GUI launcher), and they yield `undefined`, not a fallback: `path.join(undefined, x)` throws, and in the CLI it produced `''` so the data directory silently became a cwd-relative `.kabmo` (split-brain: `kabmo stop`/`status` looked in the wrong directory and could not find a live server). `os.homedir()` falls back to `getpwuid()` on POSIX and `USERPROFILE` on Windows; `os.tmpdir()` walks `TMPDIR` → `TMP` → `TEMP` then `/tmp` (and strips macOS's trailing slash). This applies to `src/cli/**` too — `src/cli/lib/path-utils.js` uses `os.homedir()` for exactly this reason. Signal numbers use the local table in `src/cli/lib/process-lifecycle.js` instead of `os.constants.signals`.
- **`node:readline`** — no Bun equivalent for generic streams; `src/server/lib/omp/rpc/lines.ts` is the in-repo NDJSON reader for Bun spawn stdout.
- **`node:zlib` brotli** — `Bun.brotliCompressSync` is `undefined`; Bun ships gzip only (`src/server/plugins/compress.ts` keeps brotli here).
- **`node:child_process`** — fully migrated (2026-09): every `exec`/`execFile`/`spawn` call site now uses `Bun.spawn`/`Bun.spawnSync`, and `readline` usage is gone. Do not reintroduce `child_process`; new code goes through `Bun.spawn` (or `lib/fs/shell.ts` for shell strings).

### Toolchain Commands: Bun Only (no npm, npx, yarn, pnpm)

**`bun.lock` is the only lockfile.** `npm`, `npx`, `yarn`, and `pnpm` are never used in this repo — not to install, not to run scripts, not to execute a binary. Every tool that would otherwise be reached through `npx` is reached through **`bunx`**, which resolves the same local `node_modules/.bin` binary.

| Never | Always |
|---|---|
| `npm install` / `npm i <pkg>` / `npm uninstall <pkg>` | `bun install` / `bun add <pkg>` / `bun remove <pkg>` (add `-d` for dev deps) |
| `npm run <script>` | `bun run <script>` (or bare `bun <script>` when it is unambiguous) |
| `npx <bin>` / `npx -y <pkg>` | `bunx <bin>` — e.g. `bunx tsc --noEmit` |
| `node <entry>` / `tsx` / `ts-node` | `bun run <entry.ts>` — Bun executes TypeScript directly |
| `npx jest` / `npx vitest` / `npx mocha` | `bun test` |

- **Never commit** `package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, or `pnpm-lock.yaml`; if one appears, delete it and re-run `bun install`.
- `bunx` is preferred over `./node_modules/.bin/...` for readability; both hit the identical local binary.

---

## Architecture & Agent Roles

### The Oh-My-Pi Autonomous Agent (`oh-my-pi`)
- **Primary Function**: Autonomous CI/CD pipeline monitoring, build log analysis, dependency resolution, and edge deployment verification.
- **Runtime Target**: Bun 1.4.x with native ESM and TypeScript striping.
- **Framework Integration**: Elysia edge routes & Rsbuild plugins.

### Kabmo Web View Interface
- **Session Sidebar** (left, default 268px): workspace folders bound to oh-my-pi projects, each listing its sessions, plus search/sort/archive toolbar and the settings/about/new-workspace/scheduler modals (`src/client/components/layout/session-sidebar/`).
- **A brand-new chat's sidebar row belongs to a CLIENT store, not to the URL — and both layouts read the same one** (`pending-sessions.ts`, `src/client/hooks/chat/omp/`). omp writes a session's `.jsonl` only when the first assistant message settles (measured ~17s, its writes are buffered), so until then the row exists only because Kabmo draws it. It used to be derived from `?sessionId=` inside the controller, which made it a property of the ACTIVE session: clicking another session deleted the row, and it reappeared seconds later when omp's scan caught up (the reported "hilang dari session sidebar dan muncul setelah beberapa saat"). And because only the desktop sidebar passed `includePendingSessions`, a phone showed no row at all until the scan landed. The store is now the single owner: `startPendingSession` mints the id at the click (every creator — both sidebars, the New Chat dialog, the plugin `openNew` bridge — writes what it returns into the URL, because an id the store does not know is a row nothing can draw), `markPendingSessionSent` is called by the send path, `adoptPendingSession` moves the entry onto the omp UUID a spawn answers with, and `hydratePendingSession` registers an id a reload (or a second tab) re-enters with an empty store — including a mid-run adopted id, whose `stream` row is the only thing that keeps its row after a switch. `withPendingRows` (`pending-rows.ts`, split out because the store hit the per-file ceiling — the store owns an entry's LIFETIME, that module owns how one is DRAWN) folds the entries in for BOTH layouts, before the search filter (a row the user's own search hides is a row the search never offered to find), forces its host folder open (desktop reads `isExpanded`; the phone seeds its drawer expansion from the same list, so a seed taken from the raw folders would collapse the one row nothing else can recover), and stamps the row from the ID's OWN clock (`pendingSessionTitle`), never `Date.now()` — a re-stamp on every revalidate advanced the title's seconds and re-shuffled `LATEST_SESSION` ordering mid-run. Retention: an authoritative row always wins, a live run holds its row wherever the user navigates, an UNSENT draft keeps its row ONLY while there is a draft to get back to (`hasComposerDraft` over the session's own UI state: blank text and no attachments retires the row, and an UNREAD slot keeps it — a blob still in flight means the answer is unknown, not empty; the OPEN chat's own row is exempt, because that is the row on screen), because its content is real — the draft lives in that session's UI state — so the row is what makes it reachable again; `MAX_UNSENT_DRAFTS` bounds how many real drafts are held, and a SENT entry is released after `PENDING_SETTLE_MS` so a child that died before writing a transcript cannot leave a row behind for the life of the tab. A plain move away from a pending chat is not an adoption: `consumeAdoption` records the `fromId → toId` pair a SPAWN produced and the session-state provider migrates only on that claim, because every switch away looks identical from the URL and the ungated version copied the pending chat's `chat.draft` into the session the user clicked next (measured). `useSessionSidebarController`'s only remaining layout option is `expandSearchMatches` (desktop); the pending row is not a per-layout request. Pinned by `pending-sessions.test.ts` (the rules), `session-sidebar-controller.test.ts` (mounted through the real provider, so a case mounted with NO options is the phone's own call) and `sidebar.e2e.ts` / `mobile.e2e.ts` (a real click, a real switch — both fail with the old URL-derived row restored).
- **The workspace list is the user's, and the omp-project sync only SEEDS it — once per database, and only while it is still empty** (`syncWorkspaceFoldersFromDiscovery`, `src/server/lib/db/workspace-sync.ts`, called once from `getDb()`). It is a bootstrap, not a background indexer: with any row present — bound or unbound, a hand-made grouping counts — it returns without touching the table, and `SYNC_WORKSPACE=false` skips it outright. Two conditions because "empty" has two meanings a row count cannot separate: a database that has never been seeded, and one whose owner deleted every folder on purpose. The `workspace_sync_ran` marker in `app_settings` covers the second, and it is written on EVERY path that decides — including the "list is non-empty, step aside" one, which is the upgrade path: an install that predates the marker arrives with folders and no marker, so skipping without recording would leave the hole open for exactly the installs that have been running longest. It is written BEFORE the insert loop — a pass that found no project still counts as run, or the first session written afterwards would seed the list. The sync's input is every session file under `~/.omp/agent/sessions`, and a session's cwd is whatever directory omp was spawned in, so without the gate each scratch directory that appeared after the user had curated their sidebar became a new folder on the next boot. Measured on the author's install: 556 session files carrying 43 distinct cwds, 33 of them scratch (`/tmp/omp-*`, `/var/folders/.../T/opencode/*`, `$HOME` itself), against a sidebar of 3 — and 88 folders already deleted, 13 of them in a single minute. The delete path is why it could not self-correct: it only tombstones the paths it saw (`deleted_workspaces`), so the next scratch cwd is a fresh insert, not a resurrection. Verified end to end on real boots against database copies: a curated 3-folder one stayed at 3 with no banner line, an emptied one seeded from discovery, one already carrying the marker stayed at 0 with the list empty, and a pre-marker 3-folder database recorded the marker on its first boot and then stayed at 0 after its folders were deleted. `workspace-sync.test.ts` pins both gates and both directions — a non-empty list is never appended to, an empty one is seeded, an emptied one is not refilled, a no-project pass still spends the one run, and a pre-marker database records it.
- **The New Workspace dialog is two tabs, and both of them write a row into that list** (`src/client/components/layout/session-sidebar/new-workspace-modal/`, reached from the toolbar's `FolderPlus` on both layouts). **Create** registers a directory that already exists; **Clone** fetches a repository and registers it in one step. Two tabs rather than one column of controls because the two flows ask for different things and neither is a variation of the other: Create needs a name and a listing to pick from, Clone needs a URL and a parent and produces its own name. The modal owns the ONE value both tabs share — the directory being browsed — so a folder entered in Create is the parent Clone shows; a second path state per tab is two fields that can disagree about where the user is.
- **The listing is read from `/api/fs/browse`, through one shared module** (`src/shared/lib/fs/browse.ts`, also used by `FolderPicker`, which `Browse…` still opens for deep navigation). Clicking a row ENTERS that folder and moves the path field with it: the dialog has no second destination, so a row can only mean "go there" and the path it sets is what a submit reads. **⟳ Refresh** re-reads the current directory; **📁+ New folder** opens an inline name field. The new folder is created, NOT selected — the listing is refreshed and the user picks it — because "make a folder" and "use this folder" are two decisions. A refusal (name taken, slashes in the name, missing parent) is reported inline and leaves the field open, since a refusal that closed it would read as success.
- **`mkdir` and `clone` are their own routes, NOT `/api/fs/action`'s `create`** (`src/server/routes/folders/{mkdir,clone}.ts`). That route scopes through `resolveRoot`, which only accepts the app root, a path beneath it, or a registered `project_path` — and the whole point of this dialog is to name a directory that is NOT registered yet (`~/projects`). A request for one would fall back to the app root and create the folder in the wrong place. So both routes validate an absolute existing parent themselves, and the folder name must be one path segment (`isSinglePathSegment`), which is what proves `join(parent, name)` cannot resolve outside the parent.
- **Clone checks the name collision BEFORE it clones, and removes the partial tree on failure.** A rejected workspace that already cost a full download is a failure the user can only undo by hand; and `git clone` creates the destination before it knows the fetch will work, so a failure leaves a partial directory in the user's projects. The workspace row is written from the RESOLVED destination — a name derived from the URL after the clone picked its own path is exactly how the row and the directory drift. `git` runs as argv with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` (`runGit`, `lib/fs/git-run.ts`), because a private repository would otherwise block on a credential prompt with no terminal to answer it and the URL is user input.
- **Both creation paths share one insert** (`registerWorkspaceFolder`, `src/server/lib/workspace/register-folder.server.ts`, used by `folders/create.ts` and `folders/clone.ts`): case-insensitive name guard, tombstone clear by CANONICAL key (a `deleted_workspaces` row for the same directory would otherwise make the new folder vanish on the next boot), the skill-discovery watcher sync, and the `sidebar-structure` signal. `isGitUrl`/`repoSlug` live in `@/shared/lib/git/url` so the Clone button's enabled state and the route's refusal read the same predicate — a per-caller copy is what lets the button enable on a string the server then rejects. Both routes refuse under `MOCK=true`.
- **Batch delete is TWO modes, not one, because the noun differs** (`src/client/hooks/workspace/batch-selection.ts` + `src/client/components/common/batch-selection/`). The checkboxes do not exist until the user asks for them — `mode` is `null` and every row is an ordinary row — because a sidebar that draws a checkbox on every row turns every click into a choice between selecting and opening. **`workspace` mode** starts from the top toolbar's `Select workspaces` (a `SIDEBAR_TOOLBAR_PRIORITY` entry, so a narrow sidebar moves it into the `⋯` menu) and deletes workspace REGISTRATIONS — the same semantics as the folder's own `Delete Workspace`, tombstone included, so the sync cannot re-add it; transcript files are untouched. **`session` mode** starts from ONE workspace's `⋯` → `Select sessions` and is SCOPED to that workspace's rows (`scopeFolderId`): its header checkbox selects the rows it offers, other folders stay ordinary lists, and "select all" is therefore unambiguous. A row in select mode toggles the selection instead of opening the session and loses its `⋯` action trigger, its roster chevron and its context menu — an action beside a checkbox is a second thing the click could mean. The header checkbox is tri-state (`indeterminate` is a DOM PROPERTY, assigned through a ref in `SelectionCheckbox` — no attribute equivalent), computed by `selectionSummary` over the rows the ARCHIVE FILTER currently offers, so "select all" can never select a row the user cannot see or unselect. **The session mode expands every folder; the workspace mode deliberately does not** — it selects folders, not rows, and expanding three trees to pick a folder is motion for nothing. Select-all and unselect-all are two buttons, not one toggle: after a partial selection the user needs both directions and one alternating control cannot say which it means. **The count and the payload are the LIVE selection** (`liveSelection`), not the raw array: a selection is deliberately never pruned as the list changes — a row can vanish for one render (a folder collapsing under a filter, a push mid-refresh) and an effect that dropped it would discard a real choice — but an id that is gone from the scope must not be counted or sent. Measured with two tabs open: one deleted a session, and the other's bar went on reading `1 of 0 sessions selected` with Delete enabled for an id that existed nowhere. **A session selection also lifts the list's pagination window** (`selectVisibleSessions` is bypassed while `sessionCheckboxes`): "select all" counts what the folder offers, and a window would tick rows with no checkbox on screen and no way to untick them — the reveal controls are hidden in that mode for the same reason. Delete is refused with nothing selected, then opens `BatchDeleteModal`, which names what actually disappears per mode and lists per-id refusals after the run — the bar's Delete never deletes on its own, and a refusal leaves the mode and its selection standing so the surviving rows can be retried. `tests/e2e/specs/batch-delete.e2e.ts` is the assembled proof (no checkbox before the mode, both entry points, a header checkbox that selects exactly its scope, a real delete that removes exactly the counted rows), and it taught two things a unit test cannot: the desktop folder trigger had no `aria-label` at all (`title` only), and a `⋯` menu row built as a `<div onClick>` is not a button to assistive tech or to `getByRole` — both now carry the role their job implies.
- **Both batch verbs are ONE request each, and they share the single-delete sequence** (`sessions/batch-delete.ts`, `folders/batch-delete.ts`). A client loop would pay N round trips and republish the sidebar N times; the server answers `{removed, refused[]}` so the dialog reports counts and reasons instead of reassembling N answers. Session removal moved into `removeSession` (`lib/omp/session/delete-flow.server.ts`) precisely so the batch cannot drift from `DELETE /api/sessions/:sessionId`: pending `new-…` refusal, `isSinglePathSegment`, the busy-child 409, `destroyAndWait`, then `purgeBtwForSession` → `purgeSessionData` — in that order. A refusal is a VERDICT, never an abort: a batch holding one mid-run session still deletes the rest. The static `batch-delete` segment is registered BEFORE the `:id` routes (an id route winning the match would turn every batch into a 405). `sidebar-toolbar.ts` gained the `select` control, so `SIDEBAR_TOOLBAR_PRIORITY` still decides what survives a narrow row.
- **Top Navbar**: active session title plus view controls — switch to mobile view, toggle the editor panel, and toggle the right panel (`src/client/components/layout/desktop-layout/TopNavbar.tsx`).
- **Both headers draw ONE control cluster, in one order** (`src/client/components/common/header-controls/`, `HeaderControls`). The two navbars are separate components — a phone's title area and a desktop titlebar have nothing in common — so the ORDER was written twice and drifted: the phone drew reload → context → sign out → panel while the desktop drew plugin slots → install → reload → sign out → editor → context → panel, and a user switching layouts had to re-find every control. The order is now stated once, in the component: **install → plugin header slots → reload → context → lock (sign out) → editor → right panel**, with each layout passing only what it has (install and the editor toggle are desktop-only; the right panel is an open-the-drawer button on the phone). The dev-only layout switch sits AFTER that list, so it cannot push a product control out of the agreed order. `PWAInstallButton` and `HeaderPanelButtons` moved out of `desktop-layout/` with it, and `withChord` (the keymap-derived tooltip suffix) moved to `src/client/lib/ui/with-chord.ts` because both headers label the same toggles. **Plugin `headerPanel` entries render on both layouts now** — the slot was documented as desktop-only, and a phone that dropped it was one more control that moved when the layout changed. Only the dropdown's geometry is per-layout: anchored under its trigger on the desktop, pinned to the viewport on the phone, which has no room for a 320px panel hanging off a control near the right edge. `header-controls/index.test.tsx` asserts the RENDERED sequence for both layouts (a table in a module would pass while a call site rendered something else), with install made visible through a real `beforeinstallprompt` and sign-out through the auth store primed the way the bootstrap primes it.
  **The cluster carries `titlebar-no-drag` ITSELF, and it draws the page-owned caption buttons on BOTH layouts — two bugs with one root cause: a shell fact stated at one call site.** `app-region` INHERITS (WebView2's own spec says so, and Chromium 152 began enforcing it), so a header marked `app-region: drag` makes every descendant a drag handle unless something below it says otherwise — and a drag handle swallows the click, silently. The desktop navbar wrapped this cluster in a `titlebar-no-drag` div and the PHONE's header did not, so on Windows every control in a phone-width window (reload, context, sign out, the right panel, `⋯`) looked right and did nothing. The marker now sits on the cluster's own root, so the next layout that draws it is correct by construction. The same reasoning moves `WindowControls` here from the desktop navbar: a desktop shell window may be NARROWER than `App`'s 768px breakpoint (all three shells allow a 720px window), so a Windows/Linux window resized down switches to the phone layout — and the phone drew no close / minimise / maximise at all, with no native title bar behind them to fall back on. macOS never reaches that branch (`drawsWindowControls` is false: its traffic lights are the system's). `shell-chrome.test.tsx` pins the rendered tree for both layouts — the cluster's class, the three buttons on the phone and at the shell's 720px minimum, their absence on macOS and in a browser, and a real click reaching `window_close` from the phone layout — because both bugs were a CALL SITE's omission, which a test reading the component would have passed.
  **A plugin header entry can be an ACTION (`opensPanel`), and a phone too narrow for it folds it into `⋯`.** `app.headerPanel({ opensPanel: 'rightPanel' | 'panel' })` names a SLOT, never a column: the host resolves it to that panel's instance key (`plugin:<id>:<slot>`) and dispatches `omp:open-panel`, and EACH LAYOUT opens it where the USER placed it — the desktop resolves the column from the dock state (`useOpenPanelRequest`, so the left dock, the right column and the editor column all work and a user who moved the panel did not break the readout), and the phone resolves the DRAWER from `phoneDrawerForPanel` (a panel placed on the left with the rail on is the left drawer; the same placement with the rail off stays in the right drawer, because `phoneRightPanels` keeps it there — the left drawer draws no strip then; an `editor`-slot takeover has no drawer at all and the click does nothing rather than opening a screen that cannot show it). The phone's two tab slots are read through `useSharedSessionState`, not the private `useSessionState`: the click writes the tab and the drawer MOUNTS on that screen change, so a private copy would open on the view it read at mount. `opensPanel` cannot be combined with `dropdown` (one click opens one thing) and naming a panel the bundle never registered is refused at registration. **The fold is measured, not guessed**: a 73px `CPU 18% / RAM 2.5G` readout in a 390px row cut the session title to 48px of its 74px, so `MOBILE_HEADER_ACTION_PX` (430) is exactly the width where the title keeps all of it — below that an ACTION entry moves into the `⋯` menu as the same action (`requestOpenPanel`), while a dropdown (needs its trigger's anchor) or a static readout (has no action) never folds. A plugin owns its trigger's markup, so the phone caps one entry at `MOBILE_HEADER_ENTRY_MAX_PX` (96) rather than letting one readout take the row. The split is resolved ONCE per header (`useHeaderEntries`) because two surfaces draw it — the row and the menu — and asking twice would be two media queries deciding one row.
  **Every header that can be the window's top strip is a title bar, and the phone's headers were not.** A desktop shell window may be narrower than `App`'s 768px layout breakpoint (macOS, Windows and Linux all allow a 720px window), so a narrowed shell window renders the PHONE layout — whose headers carried neither `data-drag-region` nor the shell's own left inset. On macOS that left the main screen with no draggable strip at all, and the drawer headers' wordmarks and hamburgers sat UNDER the 76px of traffic lights, because `titlebarPaddingLeft` reads `env(titlebar-area-x)`, which a native WKWebView never defines; the shell's width has to come from `titlebarInsetLeft`. The right drawer was worse: it had no drag region at all, so it was a screen with nothing to move the window by. Adding the attribute to a header is only half the change — `app-region` INHERITS, so the controls inside must carry `titlebar-no-drag` or they become drag handles that swallow their own clicks on Windows. `titlebar-css.test.ts` now lists all five title-bar headers (both desktop ones and the three phone ones) instead of the desktop pair, which is exactly how the gap shipped.
- **The PWA window-control inset is stated ONCE, in `src/shared/lib/ui/titlebar.ts`, because four headers draw it and the formula was wrong on macOS.** With `display_override: ["window-controls-overlay"]` the `titlebar-area-*` env variables describe the area the page may use — it runs from the frame edge to the innermost edge of the overlay housing the window controls — so the LEFT inset is `titlebar-area-x` and the RIGHT inset is what is left over AFTER the area ends: `100vw - titlebar-area-x - titlebar-area-width`. The headers computed the right inset as `100vw - titlebar-area-width`, which is the same number only when `x` is 0 (Windows/Linux, controls on the right). On macOS the traffic lights are on the LEFT (`x ≈ 76px`), so that form charged their width to the wrong edge: a measured 76px dead strip between the last navbar control and the window's right edge, which is the reported "kenapa ada jarak". `titlebarPaddingLeft`/`titlebarPaddingRight` take the base padding (and, on the phone, the notch insets) and every header uses them — the desktop navbar, the sidebar title, the phone header, the phone sessions drawer, the phone right drawer, the editor header and the full-screen diff — so a header added later cannot restate the arithmetic. `titlebar.test.ts` substitutes real macOS/Windows/tab geometries into the returned declarations and evaluates them (16 / 138 / 16 on the right, 76 / 16 / 16 on the left) rather than asserting the string, and `shell.e2e.ts` measures the last navbar control's gap to the window edge in a real browser; both fail with the old formula restored.
- **The context-usage ring is ONE component on both headers, and it is a gauge, not a dot** (`src/client/components/common/context-usage-button/`, `ContextUsageButton`). The phone had this control from the start and the desktop navbar did not, so the only way to see how close a run was to its model's window on a desktop was to open the Context panel in the right column. The ring fills CLOCKWISE with `contextPercent`, and the rotation is the SVG `transform` attribute (`rotate(-90 9 9)`) rather than a Tailwind `-rotate-90`: a CSS transform on an SVG child resolves its origin against `transform-box`, whose default differs between engines, and can pivot the arc around the viewBox corner. The stroke attributes are written KEBAB-CASE (`stroke-dasharray`) because Preact assigns a prop whose name exists as a DOM property and otherwise calls `setAttribute` — camelCase produced a `strokeDasharray` attribute no renderer reads (happy-dom exposed it; a test asserting `getAttribute` caught it). **The bands are one table** (`CONTEXT_BAND_TONES` + `contextUsageBand`, `src/shared/lib/chat/context/usage.ts`): under 50% `success`, 50–59.9% `warning`, 60% and up `error`, boundaries belonging to the worse band. Red begins well before omp's typical compaction point — `compaction.thresholdPercent` ships an 80% preset labelled "Typical threshold" — so the ring warns while there is still room to act. Verified in the browser on both layouts: 12/42% → `text-success`, 50/55% → `text-warning`, 60/72% → `text-error`, with the arc's computed `stroke` resolving to the band's colour. The source is `useSessionContextTelemetry` (`src/client/hooks/chat/context/telemetry.ts`): the `session:<id>:telemetry` topic first (so the ring moves during a run, on the same pushes the Context panel gets) with the HTTP route as the fallback for MOCK mode and in-memory sessions. The popover is the same markup on both layouts — pinned to the phone's header geometry, anchored under the trigger on the desktop — and a session with no turn says `Session not started` instead of printing zeros as if they were measurements. Pinned by `context-usage-button/index.test.tsx` (through a real realtime server) and `usage.test.ts` (the band table and the ring geometry).
- **Chat Timeline** (center): the streaming agent conversation — thinking accordions, tool-call cards, rendered mermaid diagrams (click one to open the zoom/pan viewer in `src/client/components/common/diagram-viewer/`), queue panel, and the composer (`src/client/components/workspace/chat-timeline/`).
- **The composer's three Enter bindings are SETTINGS, and they live in the `kabmo_settings` blob** (`resolveComposerKeybindings`, `src/shared/lib/chat/composer/keybindings.ts`). Settings → Chats → Keybindings persists `keybindingSend` / `keybindingNewLine` / `keybindingSteering` through `writeKabmoSettings`, so they nest under `kabmo_settings` — NOT at the top level of `appSettings`, which is the raw `app_settings` row map. The handler used to read each one as `appSettings?.keybindingSend`, found nothing, and fell back to its default, so the panel showed a choice the composer ignored: measured in the browser, with "send" set to Ctrl+Enter a bare Enter still cleared the draft and started a turn. `readKabmoSetting` resolves the blob and top-level keys alike (the same helper the composer already used for `goalDefaultBudget`), and the file's own defaults mirror the settings editor's so an unset key reads as the binding the panel renders. Resolution happens per KEYPRESS, not per render, which is what makes a binding changed in Settings apply to the very next Enter with no event and no reload — the composer has nothing to re-render for the next keydown to observe it. `keybindings.test.ts` pins the blob as the source (including that the blob beats a stale top-level copy), and the defaults.
- **The composer stretches from a control at the trailing edge of its top row, and "bigger" means two different things per layout** (`chat-input/expansion.ts` is the decision table; the button is `Maximize composer` / `Restore composer` in `AttachmentToolbar`). **A phone takes the whole viewport** — the card becomes `fixed inset-0 z-40`, so every dialog stays above it (extension/ask `z-50`, goal and undo `z-[60]`, plan review `z-[70]`, toasts `z-[200]`; a stretched composer over a blocking dialog would make the agent's question unanswerable) and the soft keyboard is lifted by the card's OWN overlap with the visible area (`useKeyboardInset(cardRef, expandable && expanded)` — measured 336px of an 844px viewport with a stubbed `visualViewport`, which put the Send button's bottom at 500 against a visible bottom of 508). **A desktop grows IN PLACE** — `min-h-[38vh]` on the textarea (measured 342px at a 900px viewport) with the card still in the dock at its panel width, because a full-screen composer there would hide the transcript the prompt is written against. Three rules are load-bearing and each was a bug first. **The card's POSITION is a field of its own, never a class in the base list**: Tailwind orders its position utilities `static, fixed, absolute, relative, sticky`, so a `relative` in the base string WINS over a `fixed` added later whatever the order they are written in — measured, the phone card reported `position: relative` with `fixed inset-0 z-40` sitting on it and stayed in the dock, which a unit test on the returned class cannot see (only a browser resolves which class wins). **The popup flips over the draft on a stretched phone** (`ComposerPickerPlacement`: `up` is the docked `bottom-full mb-1`, `over` is `top-2` inside the textarea's own wrapper, which `ComposerTextarea.wrapperClassName` makes the flex member that owns the leftover height) — a full-height composer's top edge IS the screen's, so an upward list is drawn off the viewport with nothing to scroll back. **A DISPATCHED send collapses the stretch on BOTH layouts, and Escape collapses either** (a document `keydown` listener, so it works with the popup closed; with the popup OPEN the picker eats the first Escape and the second collapses). The collapse is driven by the send pipeline's `onSent` (`useComposerPipeline`), never by the click handler: it was `if (isMobile) setExpanded(false)` in `handleSubmit`, so a desktop kept a 38vh box over the conversation the prompt was written against, and on either layout a press that dispatched NOTHING (an empty draft, an attachment whose read produced no body) collapsed a box whose content had not been sent — a refusal that read as success. `onSent` runs after every guard and immediately before `onSend`, so the button, Enter, Cmd+Enter steering and a queued follow-up all collapse it and a refused send does not. The control is spelled `Maximize`, not `Expand`, because `chat.e2e.ts` collects every `[aria-label^="Expand "]` and requires each to name a TOOL — a second control spelled that way read as a tool card titled `composer`. `expandable` gates it: the chat composer and the pending-session composer have it, the New Chat modal (which maximizes its own card) and the BTW form (sized by the panel it sits in) do not. Pinned by `expansion.test.ts` and, for the class-order trap a unit test cannot see, `tests/e2e/specs/composer-stretch.e2e.ts` plus the stretch spec in `mobile.e2e.ts`.
- **A turn is steered, never cancelled, and the binding governs which key does it.** Ctrl/Cmd+Enter (or whichever `keybindingSteering` names) submits through `steerOmpAgent` → `OmpAgentHandle.steerOmpRun` → `POST /api/agent/:id {type:'steer'}`, which delivers INTO the running turn (omp answers one `agent_start`/`agent_end` pair; `abort_and_prompt` produces two and throws the watched answer away). Verified against the real spawn path with the scripted child: the frame reaching the child is `{"type":"steer","message":"…"}`, a steer while the session is idle is refused `400 session_idle`, and `followUpBehavior: 'steering'` makes even a plain mid-run Enter steer instead of queueing.
- **A running subagent is steerable and stoppable, from the roster and from its transcript.** `cancel_subagent` / `steer_subagent` are omp RPC verbs that act on ONE subagent without touching the parent turn, wired through `sessions/subagent-control.ts` (POST-only, id validated against the same grammar the transcript path derivation uses) and the `PASSTHROUGH_COMMANDS` set. The id is the ROSTER id the session file and `get_subagents` both report — measured on 18.8.3 it is a slug (`Sleeper`, `ListOmpws`), NOT a uuid, and the `subagent_progress` frame carries no `id` at all, so the roster is the only source. Both routes answer `409 session_not_running` for a session this process does not manage, because a subagent is by definition something the session's own child runs — the spawn path must never boot a child to answer "stop that". The UI is `SubagentControls` (a steer box plus a stop button, rendered in place of the transcript's composer ONLY while the subagent runs) and a stop button on each roster row, which is a SIBLING of the row's own button rather than nested inside it (a button in a button is invalid and swallows the inner click).
- **The subagent transcript renders the chat's own footer, in whichever variant the chat is in.** `SubagentView` passes `isMobile` down to `RunFooter`, so a phone gets the same compact row the main agent gets — provider • model • thinking inline, everything else (date, duration, tokens) behind the three-dot sheet — instead of the desktop row it used to draw. `showActions={false}` (and `userActions={false}` on its message rows) is the same rule the BTW panel follows: Retry / New-chat / Undo act on the CHAT, and this view owns no chat turn, so they would be dead buttons. **The thinking level must come from the CHILD, never the parent session** — a subagent routinely runs at a different level than the chat that spawned it. Two sources, because the two paths carry different data: a live child reports `progress.resolvedThinkingLevel` (omp's `AgentProgress`; `parseSubagentProgress` reads it), while an on-disk transcript records it as a `thinking_level_change` entry that `convertMessages` walks and stamps onto each non-user row (mirrors the server's `loadSessionMessages`). Note the frame shape: `subagent_progress` nests the `AgentProgress` under `payload.progress` and puts no `id` on the wrapper, so `readSubagentProgressFrame` unwraps it — reading the wrapper alone silently dropped every field the frame reports, including the resolved model. That reader is shared by the roster, the transcript hook and the server's liveness fold.
- **Editor Panel**: opened-file tabs and code editing (`src/client/components/workspace/editor/`).
- **Both editors draw the same type, from one module** (`src/shared/lib/code/editor/typography.ts`). The desktop editor and the phone's full-screen editor are the same surface on two layouts, so face and line box are shared while padding and gutter width stay per-layout. They were not: the stack was written out as a literal twice in the desktop editor and replaced by a bare `monospace` on the phone, which dropped Fira Code and put the phone's line numbers in a different face from the code beside them — the gutter was already `font-mono`. Two further drifts went with it: the phone used a px `line-height` (`20px`) that does not scale with the zoom control, so at 18px the rows were cramped and at 10px loose, and it pinned the gutter to `text-[10px]` and a fixed `w-10` while the code scaled, clipping a 3-digit line number at the top of the range. The line box is a ratio (`EDITOR_LINE_HEIGHT`) applied to the editor **and** its gutter — with word wrap off the gutter rows carry no explicit height and take their spacing from their own line-height, so a gutter pinned to a different value drifts out of register with the lines it counts. The zoom bounds are shared too (8–24px), because a phone clamped to a narrower range is a different editor, not a smaller one. Verified in the browser on the CodeMirror surface: at 12px both layouts measure `Fira Code / 12px / 18px` on `.cm-content`, `.cm-line`, `.cm-gutters` and a gutter element, and zooming to 18px moves all four to `18px / 27px`; the clamp stops at 8 and 24 on both. The type is INHERITED by the editor rather than restated in a CM6 theme: the host element carries family, size and line box, and `.cm-editor` inherits — a theme extension carrying a pixel size would have to be reconfigured on every zoom step, and a reconfigure re-creates the editor's plugins (including the tokenizer).
- **The editor surface IS CodeMirror 6, and Shiki stayed the tokenizer.** `code-surface/` (`index.tsx` + `extensions.ts`, `handle.ts`, `shiki.ts`, `keymap.ts`, `theme.ts`, `documents.ts`, `marks.ts`, `fold.ts`, `bracket-guide.ts`, `stream-language.ts`) replaced a transparent `<textarea>` over a Shiki-highlighted `<pre>` plus Kabmo's own windowing, wrap measurement, undo stack, multi-cursor replication, find matcher and gutter geometry — measured on a 4 800-line file, one keystroke cost **59.6 ms** median before and **0.2 ms** after, flat from 8 000 to 20 000 lines (the surface renders ~54 `.cm-line` nodes at 20 000). **The palette is not CM6's**: a lezer grammar build would cover 48 of the 59 ids `shiki-langs` knows and paint a second palette, while the chat timeline, diff panel, wiki reader and markdown preview all render through `highlightCode` — so a `ViewPlugin` tokenizes `view.viewport` (+40 context lines, 12 000-char line bail-out) and emits `Decoration.mark` carrying Shiki's `--shiki-light`/`--shiki-dark` pair, which makes a theme switch CSS-only. **`CodeSurfaceHandle` is unchanged**, which is what kept the find widget, outline jump, line jump and search reveal working without knowing about CM6. Three facts in that stack are load-bearing and each was a bug first. **The per-tab `EditorState` cache** (`documents.ts`, applied with `view.setState`) is what gives a tab its own caret and UNDO HISTORY — a single buffer meant ⌘Z after a tab switch undid an edit in the file the user was no longer looking at — and it is LRU-bounded at 12, because a history holds whole-document snapshots. **`languageData` must return an object KEYED by the data name**: `languageDataAt` looks the name up in each provider's result, so returning the tokens bare (`() => [{ line: '//' }]`) yields an empty list, `toggleComment` returns `false`, and ⌘/ silently does nothing — measured in TypeScript and YAML with no error anywhere. **CM6's `defaultKeymap` is appended after the generated bindings**: it is what makes Enter, Backspace, arrows, Home/End and word-wise movement work at all, and appending it is what keeps a chord the table claims from being shadowed. Read-only is BOTH facets (`readOnly` stops the commands, `editable(false)` stops typing); a viewer takes both. **`applyDocument` must NOT be silenced as an external write**: the `writingRef` guard exists so a write that came FROM the parent does not echo back through `onValueChange`, and a find-bar replace is a USER edit — suppressing it left the parent holding the pre-replace buffer, so the autosave never saw the change and the next render compared the stale `value` against the document and wrote the OLD text back (measured: replace-all, switch tab, come back → the original text, and nothing on disk).
- **A stream language makes bracket matching token-aware, and the pair GUIDE draws the span the highlight cannot.** `stream-modes.ts` maps 48 of the 59 Shiki ids to a `@codemirror/legacy-modes` mode, loaded lazily through **literal** `import()` specifiers and installed into a per-document `languageCompartment`. **Lezer grammars were measured and rejected**: `javascriptLanguage` alone is +39 KB gzip, the whole `lang-*` set covers 24 of 59 languages for +439 KB, and — the decisive fact — a lezer tree is LAZY, so bracket matching is WRONG until the parse catches up (3 wrong pairs at mount on a 176-line file, and 2 still wrong on a 337-line one after a full parse, `Toolbar.tsx:97`). A stream mode is ~1 KB, and its tree exists synchronously: the same 176-line file is 0 wrong pairs at mount. **The loader must be a literal specifier, one `import()` per entry** — a template literal (`import(\`…/mode/${name}\`)`) builds fine and then NEVER resolves at runtime in the dev server, which rewrites it to `hmr.dynamicImport('@codemirror/legacy-modes/mode/${ref.module}')` with the placeholder unsubstituted. Measured in the browser: the mode silently failed to load, the surface stayed textual, and no console error was logged. The pattern is the repo's own `LANG_LOADERS`. **`STREAM_MODE_EXPORTS` is a second table because the export name is not derivable from the Shiki id** — `scss` is `sCSS`, `objective-c` is `objectiveC`, `dockerfile` is `dockerFile`, and `javascript`/`clike`/`sql` each ship several modes from one module. **The compartment is installed BEFORE the manual `languageData` table**, because `languageDataAt` returns every provider's answer in order and the commands take the FIRST: measured, a wrong manual `##` entry won and commented a Rust line until the mode went first. **`bracket-guide.ts` draws the vertical rule from the caret's brace to its pair** (`Decoration.line` per row, `--bracket-guide-col` carrying the column of the pair's LEFTMOST brace — one straight line cannot meet two braces in different columns, and `const v = merge(base, {` opens at 30 while its closer sits at 0, so taking the minimum is what makes the rule land on a brace rather than float above nothing. The column is `countColumn` cells, not code units, because CM6 writes `tab-size: state.tabSize` on `.cm-content`; and the CSS offset adds `--surface-pad` back, because an absolutely-positioned `::before` resolves against the line's PADDING box while the glyph starts inside it — measured before that fix: the rule sat 16px left of the brace it named). Blank lines get no guide (the gap is the signal), a one-line pair gets none (nothing to connect), and a span past `MAX_GUIDE_LINES` (400) is refused rather than drawn. It reads the same `matchBrackets` call `bracketMatching()` makes, so the line and the highlight cannot disagree. Verified in Chromium: on a 16-line nested file the guide covers 11 rows at column 37 with `--theme-info` at 45%, and with the string `const s = "}";` inside the block the guide runs PAST it to the real closing brace; a caret placed exactly on that string brace paints **no** highlight and **no** guide, which is the token-aware answer the textual matcher cannot give. Pinned by `bracket-guide.test.ts` (the pair, the columns, the blank-line and cap rules, every mode loader resolving to a real mode object, and the two language sets covering all 59 ids) and `stream-language.test.ts` (the compartment keeps the document and the undo history, and the mode's tokens come first).

- **Folding and bracket matching ship WITHOUT a grammar, and that choice is what makes them work in 59 languages instead of 19.** `code-surface/fold.ts` registers a `foldService` that answers from INDENTATION (vim's `foldmethod=indent`): a line folds up to the last following line indented deeper than it, blank lines never end a block, and the opener stays visible because the range starts at `line.to`. `foldGutter()` — not `codeFolding()` — is the entry point, because the gutter brings the fold STATE field with it: a `foldEffect` dispatched into a state that has no `foldState` is discarded with no error (measured: no placeholder, no throw), which is the failure that would look like a working gutter that does nothing. `bracketMatching()` is the other half and it is TEXTUAL here, which is a trade stated rather than hidden: with no lezer tree it pairs by scanning, so `const a = "{"` and `const c = "}"` are paired 28 characters apart. A token-aware answer needs a grammar (115 KB gzip for `lang-javascript` alone) and `matchBrackets` costs 0.008–0.04 ms per caret move, so the wrong answer is cheap and confined to braces inside strings. **The cap on the fold scan is a cost bound, and it REFUSES rather than truncates.** The scan walks forward until the indentation dedents, so a generated JSON that is one 500 000-line block would be walked on every gutter line the reader scrolls past — measured 74.6 ms per call unbounded, 0.50 ms with `MAX_FOLD_LINES` (5 000). Past the cap the answer is `null` (no marker, no fold), because a range ending at an arbitrary line is a fold the reader never chose; a block that reaches the END of the document is not capped and still folds. **Two theme facts were measured, and each fails silently.** The bracket rules must spell the focused form `&.cm-focused .cm-matchingBracket` — the package's own is `.ͼ1.cm-focused .cm-matchingBracket` (specificity 0,2,0), so a bare `.cm-matchingBracket` here is (0,1,1) and loses, leaving CM6's hardcoded `#328c8252` in a themed app. And `border: 1px solid var(--x)` is a shorthand style-mod tries to EXPAND and cannot parse — it emits `border-width: var(--x); border-style: var(--x)`, which paints nothing — so `cm-foldPlaceholder` declares `borderWidth`/`borderStyle`/`borderColor` separately. Read-only viewers still fold (verified), folds survive a per-tab `setState` round trip and map through edits, and the four chords are CM6's own `foldKeymap` rows (`⌥⌘[` / `⌥⌘]` / `Ctrl-Alt-[` / `Ctrl-Alt-]`), added to `EDITOR_KEY_BINDINGS` and `PALETTE_ORDER` so the palette cannot advertise a chord nothing binds. Verified in a real Chromium against an isolated MOCK server: the fold gutter renders 4 markers on a 16-line nested file, a click took 17 rendered lines to 5 with the `…` placeholder, clicking the placeholder restored all 17, `⌥⌘[` folded and `⌥⌘]` unfolded (including the `“` character macOS actually reports for Option+`[`), and the caret beside a `{` painted both `{` and `}` with the background resolving to `--theme-info` (`rgb(2,132,199)`) rather than CM6's hex. Pinned by `fold.test.ts` (the rule, including the cap and the per-language samples), `fold-stack.test.ts` (the mounted stack: the state field, CM6's `foldable()` agreeing, a real gutter marker, a read-only fold, and the bracket classes) and `theme.test.ts` (the focused-rule specificity and the longhand declarations).
- **`useFileTabs` closes the previous workspace's tabs on a root CHANGE, and on nothing else.** The editor column's tab list is per session and restored from the blob, so the wipe exists for exactly one event: the user moved to another workspace, whose files must not stay open. Two other arrivals look like that event and are not, and each cost a measured bug. **A `null` root is not a root** — `activeProjectPath` is derived from the sidebar's folder list, which is empty for a beat on every load and again whenever the structure topic re-subscribes, so wiping on it closed the tabs the user had just opened (the reported "the file I clicked vanished and the editor says Select a file to open", and the rare `outline.e2e.ts` failure). **The FIRST known root is not a change either** — it is the workspace the page was already showing, so wiping on it discarded the tabs the session blob had just restored; that is why `editor-scroll.e2e.ts` had to gate every tree click on the `/state` response, and why a click that lost that race left the editor blank. `file-tabs.test.ts` pins both directions (a transient `null` keeps the tabs, a different root closes them).
- **A blob that arrives after a write made DURING its read must not discard that write.** `loadSession` merges local keys over the response for two separate reasons and needs both: `dirtySessions` covers a write whose persist has not landed, while a write whose persist HAS landed clears that flag and would otherwise be adopted away by a response computed before it — measured as a file opened during the page's first `/state` read coming back with `layout.openedFiles` gone. The store therefore records a monotonic counter per `(session, key)` write and merges exactly the keys written after the read STARTED; a key the local side no longer holds was CLEARED in that window, so the response's copy is deleted rather than restored. `store.test.ts` pins the three shapes (written, untouched, cleared).
- **A dropped file OPENS; it is never inserted into the buffer — and that needed a capture-phase claim, because CodeMirror handles `drop` itself.** `handlers.drop` in `@codemirror/view` reads each dropped file's TEXT and inserts it at the pointer, then calls `preventDefault` — so the panel's own `onDrop` saw `defaultPrevented` and stood down. Measured in the browser: dropping `b.ts` on an open `a.ts` put `b.ts`'s text at the top of the buffer and the debounced autosave wrote it to disk, while the overlay had promised "Drop a file to open it". `useEditorDrop` therefore also returns `captureDropProps` (`onDropCapture`), spread on the same element as `dropProps`; it claims ONLY a drag over `[data-code-surface]` (the attribute `CodeSurface` sets on its host), so a drop on the composer stays the composer's and a text drag is still CodeMirror's to insert. `use-editor-drop.test.ts` pins all three directions (claimed on a surface, untouched elsewhere, a `text/plain` drag ignored).
- **A dropped file opens on a desktop shell too, and there the page has to ASK the shell for the path.** Measured on macOS 27 by delivering a real Finder pasteboard into a `WKWebView`: `types ["Files"]`, `files [File]`, `getData('text/uri-list')` and `getData('text/plain')` both empty, `File.path` undefined, and `DataTransferItem.getAsFileSystemHandle` undefined. Both of `useEditorDrop`'s routes are therefore dead there — WebKit implements no File System Access API (bug 257792), and it stopped adding local filenames as URLs to a file drag (changeset 222257), so `/api/fs/scope` is never asked either — and every Finder drop on the macOS shell was refused with "that drop carried no readable file" while the SAME drop attached fine in the composer, which only needs the bytes. That asymmetry is why it read as an editor bug rather than a missing capability. `DropWebView` (`packages/macos/Sources/KabmoDesktop/DroppedPaths.swift`) reads `NSFilenamesPboardType` (falling back to `public.file-url`) in `performDragOperation` **before** calling `super`, and the page asks for it with `dropped_paths` (`client/lib/desktop/dropped-paths.ts`); measured, the injection lands ahead of the DOM event so the handler sees the answer. Three rules are load-bearing. **`super` is still called and its value is the return** — the page needs the DOM event to know WHERE the drop landed (editor column, composer, code surface) and the composer attaches from the payload's bytes, so swallowing the drag would fix the editor by breaking the chat. **The answer is CONSUMED once** (and cleared by `draggingExited`): a drag released over no target leaves the record unread, and answering it to the NEXT drop reopens a file the user abandoned. **The page age-checks it** (`SHELL_DROP_TTL_MS`, 2s) because it asks in the drop's own turn, so anything older is a leftover. The other two shells do NOT need it and do not implement it — WebKitGTK preloads `text/uri-list` for a file-manager drag, and WebView2 is Chromium where the file-system handle exists — so the bridge read fails there and the drop's own payload decides. Pinned by `use-editor-drop.shell.test.ts` (the shell path opens the file, a handle still wins, an in-page drag is still reported, a stale answer is refused) and `packages/macos/TestsWindow/DroppedPathsWindowTests.swift` (a real drag into the real `DropWebView`: the DOM event still arrives with bytes and no URI list, the shell captured the path, the second ask answers nothing, and a drag that leaves clears it).
- **The find widget's count and the bytes Replace writes come from ONE regex compiler, and the flags are the reason.** The scan runs through CodeMirror's `RegExpCursor`, which compiles the pattern source with `gm` (+ `u`), while `buildFindRegex` used a bare `g`/`gi` — so the two ends answered differently on ordinary queries. Measured before the fix: `^foo` counted 2 matches on a three-line file and Replace All rewrote only the first (no `m`); `\p{L}\d` matched three times and replaced ZERO (without `u` a property escape is a literal `p`); `\-` was called invalid by the scan while Replace All edited the file with the non-unicode reading. `BASE_FLAGS` (`gm` + `u` where the engine has it) is now stated once in `find-scan.ts` and every path reads it, and a zero-width match is skipped by the write exactly as it is uncounted by the scan (`^` under "No results" used to insert at every line start). `find-replace.test.ts` asserts the two ends together rather than one engine in isolation.
- **`insertLineAbove` keeps the line's indentation.** CM6 has no command for it, so the surface implements it — and a bare newline above an indented line left the caret at column 0, putting the next typed character outside the block. The new line carries the current line's own leading whitespace and the caret lands after it, which is the rule the textarea surface applied and what CM6's own `insertBlankLine` derives from `IndentContext` for "below". `keymap.test.ts` drives it through `runSurfaceCommand` (the palette's own path in) against a `{state, dispatch}` stand-in.
- **The phone's editor is `select-text`.** The full-screen sheet's root is `select-none` (it is a header-and-status layout), and the code surface is now a `contenteditable` whose selection and caret belong to the browser — an inherited `user-select: none` is the one thing that can take both away on a device. The desktop pane already carried the class; the phone's surface did not.
- **A text file's ceiling is 32 MiB, and it was measured rather than chosen.** The old number was 5 MiB and its justification died with the `<textarea>` it was written for ("well below the size at which a textarea stops responding"). Re-measured on the assembled app — a generated source file, Files panel open, Chromium: 5 MB opens in 99 ms at 17 ms/keystroke, 10 MB 147/32, 20 MB 212/34, 30 MB 323/39, 40 MB 283/45, **50 MB 372/72 with a 1 078 MB JS heap for ONE tab**, and 64 MB never finishes loading. The curve is flat to 40 and breaks at 50, so 32 MiB is the last size with a comfortable margin below the knee. `EDITOR_MAX_BYTES_BY_EXT` still overrides per extension and the two document ceilings are INDEPENDENT of it — the text one is now ABOVE the PDF one, which is fine because they answer different questions (a text buffer is held for the life of a tab and re-posted on every save; a PDF is streamed to Chrome's viewer and never buffered) rather than ranking a shared budget. A test that writes `EDITOR_MAX_FILE_BYTES + 1` and expects the PDF branch must use a PDF-sized number instead: "past text, under PDF" stopped existing. `editor.e2e.ts` opens a real 6 MB file and saves an edit, so the constant and the route cannot drift.
- **Two costs had to come out before that ceiling was reachable, and both were linear-in-file-size work paid PER KEYSTROKE.** (1) `syncExternal` compared the parent's `value` against `doc.toString()` on every render — a full stringify of the document, and the parent re-renders on every keystroke, so a large file paid it twice per character (measured in Bun: 7.2 ms at 20 MB, 13.7 ms at 30 MB). It now compares against `lastValueRef`, which the update listener and the function itself keep in step with what the DOCUMENT holds, so the usual case is one reference check; the browser showed the knee moving 67 → 39 ms at 30 MB and 83 → 45 ms at 40 MB. (2) The outline scanned AND rendered every declaration on every keystroke — see the bullet below.
- **The outline is bounded twice, because a large file's outline is not a long list, it is a DOM.** Measured on a 10 MB source with 63 618 declarations: **446 308 DOM nodes, 415 MB of JS heap, and 953 ms per keystroke** while the Files panel was on screen (the editor's own cost is 34 ms), with 2.7 s to expand the section. `shared/lib/code/outline/rows.ts` holds both bounds, and each answers a different question. **`MAX_OUTLINE_ROWS` (500) caps the ROWS**, with the count badge reading `500/63,618` and a line under the list naming what is not shown — an outline is a map you scan or jump from, so the window is the TOP of the file in source order, never a sample. **`MAX_OUTLINE_SCAN_CHARS` (8 MiB) skips the SCAN**, because it is one blocked stretch of main thread after each typing pause (38 ms at 1 MB, 160 at 5, 262 at 10, 519 at 20) and half a second per pause is not worth a list nobody reads; the panel says the file is too large to outline instead of showing an empty list, which would read as "no declarations here". **The scan is also DEBOUNCED** (`useOutlineView`, 300 ms): it walks the whole buffer and the buffer changes on every keystroke, so a live scan charged the walk to typing (704 ms/keystroke at 10 MB live against 34 ms without). A different DOCUMENT scans immediately — rows from the previous file point at lines of a document that is gone — and the timer is not armed at all past the scan ceiling, where the sentence never changes. Verified end to end: 10 MB with 63 618 symbols went from 953 ms/keystroke and 446 308 nodes to 33 ms and 4 495 nodes, and 20 MB from 519 ms of blocked main thread per pause to none.
- **A markdown preview turns the outline into a TABLE OF CONTENTS, and the mode is the document's own `preview` flag.** The list is one component (`OutlineSection`) fed by one published document (`EditorDocument.preview`), and what a row DOES depends on which of the two views is on screen: in the SOURCE it is a symbol outline whose row publishes a `LineRevealRequest` (caret on the declaration, name selected); in a RENDERED markdown page it is a contents entry whose row publishes a `HeadingRevealRequest` and scrolls the page to that heading — **the reader stays in the preview**. The previous behaviour forced the source view open to satisfy a line request, which threw the reader out of the document they were reading (the reported "kenapa pindah ke edit"). Two stores, not one with a mode flag: the requests name different things and are served by different surfaces, and one editor holds both at once (a markdown tab toggles between them) — the same reason `line-reveal` is separate from `search-reveal`. **The heading match is by NORMALIZED text plus OCCURRENCE** (`shared/lib/markdown/headings.ts`): the row's name is the source's spelling (`Hello **world**`) and the rendered heading prints `Hello world`, so a raw comparison matches nothing; duplicate `## Notes` headings are ordinary and a plain find always answers the first, so the nth row goes to the nth heading. **The ordinal is the FALLBACK, never the first choice** — inline markup that normalizes differently from what the renderer printed is one heading off rather than an inert click. `findHeadingIndex` is the single matcher, used by the desktop panel's jump (`useHeadingJump`) and the phone's sheet (`MobileOutlineButton`), because a second copy is how one of them lands on the first `Notes` forever. **The scroll is a RECT delta, not `offsetTop`**: the preview scroller is `overflow-auto` but not positioned, so a heading's `offsetTop` measures against whichever ancestor happens to be positioned. It also cancels the tab's pending scroll restore (`cancelScrollRestore`), or the replay that runs for ~600 ms after the document opens writes the remembered offset back over the jump. **A request neither the surface nor the document can serve yet stays PENDING, never cleared**: a source view answers line requests, and the preview's headings land a frame after its scroller, so clearing on "not now" loses a click that was about to be servable — the rule `useLineJump` already applies to a missing surface. The row's accessible name drops its line number in a preview (`Heading Second Section`, not `… , line 9`): a rendered document has none to name, and the number would describe a document the reader cannot see. Pinned by `headings.test.ts` (normalization, occurrence, ordinal fallback), `use-heading-jump.test.ts` (which heading was chosen, where `scrollTop` landed, the pending rules), `use-editor-document.test.ts` (only a MARKDOWN preview publishes the flag — a CSV grid and an SVG picture are rendered too but their rows are not headings), `OutlineSection.test.tsx` and `OutlineButton.test.tsx` (both surfaces, both modes), and `outline.e2e.ts` + `mobile-editor.e2e.ts` (a real scroll in a real preview, with the reader still in the preview afterwards).

- **A failed read is reported, never substituted, and a file's ceiling is enforced at the route too.** `EDITOR_MAX_BYTES_BY_EXT` is read by the classifier, by the refusal sentence AND by `/api/fs/raw`, so no entry point can bypass the limit and no two surfaces can name different numbers (`unsupportedEditorMessage` derives its number from the same table, and the tests derive their expectation from the constant rather than spelling "5.00 MB").
- **Every editor and diff scroll container remembers where it was left, per tab and per surface** (`src/shared/lib/workspace/scroll-positions.ts` + `src/client/hooks/editor/use-scroll-restore.ts`). The code surface's scroller is CodeMirror's own (`view.scrollDOM`) and a diff's is the merge view's element, so neither is a node the panel renders — the hook takes the element as an OPTION (`element`) rather than reading a `ref`, and the surface reports it through `onScroller` on mount. The editor column still swaps ONE container's document on a tab switch, so the browser clamped the shared scroller to the height of whatever arrived next — measured: leaving a 400-line file at line ~40 and returning landed at line 1. The offsets live in the session's UI state (`editor.scrollPositions`, so they survive a reload), and the phone editor keeps its own slot (`editor.mobileScrollPositions`) because it is keyed by PATH while the desktop column is keyed by tab id — one map would let the desktop's prune drop the phone's entries. Three rules make it work, each a bug if dropped: **the restore REPLAYS for ~600 ms**, because a file's height is not final on the commit that opens it (the line geometry is measured in a layout effect and the panel animates its width) and a single write is clamped to the height that existed then; **a reader's own scroll cancels the pending restore**, since fighting the wheel is worse than a missed restore; and **the offset is recorded from scroll EVENTS, never by reading the element in the effect's cleanup** — a layout effect's cleanup runs after the DOM already holds the next tab's document, so reading `element.scrollTop` there stores the NEW file's offset under the OLD tab's key. A restore's own write is excluded by a `writingRef` flag, not by comparing `scrollTop` to the read-back value: a scroll event can be delivered SYNCHRONOUSLY from the assignment (measured in happy-dom, and a browser may deliver it before the next frame), when the read-back has not run yet and the delta looks like the reader's — that dropped the pending restore and left a growing document at its clamp. The container is armed even when nothing is stored, because "no entry" means the top and the shared container still carries the previous document's offset. Surfaces are separate offsets (`code`/`rendered` for a file tab, `unified`/`split` for a diff): a markdown preview and its source, and the two diff row shapes, share neither a line nor a height. A closed tab's offset is dropped WHERE THE TAB CLOSES (`dropScrollPosition`, called from `useFileTabs`' close / diff-conversion / workspace-wipe paths) — **never from an effect watching `layout.openedFiles`**, which sees that list at moments that are not user actions (before the session's blob has restored the tabs, mid workspace switch) and deleted every stored position on the way in: measured, the offset persisted and then came back as `{}` after a reload. The hook reads the session context OPTIONALLY: the shared diff views also draw a chat tool card, and a caller may mount one without the provider. **The element is handed in, not read through a `ref`**: the code surface's scroller is CodeMirror's (`view.scrollDOM`) and appears only once the view mounts, so the surface reports it through `onScroller` and the caller passes it as the hook's `element` option — which also re-arms the restore on the render it arrives in. `tests/e2e/specs/editor-scroll.e2e.ts` pins the round trip and the reload, and `mobile-editor.e2e.ts` the phone's (a real browser clamping a real container — happy-dom computes no layout, so the unit test's geometry is faked).
- **The face itself is a setting, and only one of the offered faces is bundled** (`EditorFontSection`, `EDITOR_FONT_CHOICES`). `editorFontStack` puts the chosen family FIRST in the shared stack, so a face the device does not have falls through to the bundled Fira Code rather than replacing it — verified: an uninstalled family measures identically to Fira Code, not to a system serif. That property is why the picker can offer JetBrains Mono / SF Mono / Menlo / Consolas / Monaco / Courier New, none of which the app ships, and why the section marks Fira Code `Bundled` and previews each row in the face it names (an absent choice shows its own fallback before the user commits to it). A change has to reach an editor that is **already open**, which rules out reading `appSettings` — a boot-time prop the editor only sees again after a reload — so the picker broadcasts `omp:editor-font-changed` and `useEditorFontStack` subscribes, the same shape the theme uses; the event fires only when the value actually moved, or every unrelated settings toggle would churn every editor's stack. Verified live in the browser with no reload: the open editor's textarea, `<pre>` and gutter all moved from `Menlo` to `Courier New` in one step and stayed aligned, and the choice survived a full reload.
- **The editor's COLOUR theme is a second axis beside the app palette** (`EditorThemeSection`, `editorTheme` in `kabmo_settings`, `shared/lib/code/editor/themes.ts`). `auto` is the default and means "the app palette" — nothing changes for an install that never opens the picker — while any other value is a Shiki theme id that paints the code surface's own background, ink, gutter, selection, caret, find bands and diff bands. The separation is the feature: a reader who works in Dracula or Nord keeps that inside the editor while the console around it stays on the app theme.
  **The catalogue is GENERATED, and its gate is what keeps it honest.** `scripts/editor-themes.mjs` writes `themes.generated.ts` from the installed `@shikijs/themes` (65 themes) — surface, ink, gutter, selection, cursor, find, border, the two diff bands and three syntax accents for the picker's swatch — so the grid draws every card without fetching a theme module (1.3 MB of TextMate JSON). `bun run editor:themes:check` is a CI gate, and `themes.test.ts` re-derives every row from the package, so a `@shikijs/themes` bump fails a test instead of shipping swatches the highlighter no longer has. **A key a theme omits is a `color-mix` of its OWN ink and surface** — 46 themes ship no `editorGutter.background` and 8 no `editor.selectionBackground`, and borrowing another theme's colour would make the swatch a lie.
  **Two mechanisms, and confusing them is the bug.** The CHROME is `--editor-*` custom properties written on the surface's host (`editorThemeVars`), with `--theme-*` as the fallback in every declaration — so `auto` is a no-op, and switching between two editor themes is a pure repaint with no transaction at all. The TOKENS are baked into the Shiki decoration plugin, because Shiki is handed theme NAMES at tokenize time: that one is a COMPARTMENT reconfigure (`themeCompartment`), which keeps the document, the selection and the undo history. An explicit theme names ITSELF in both Shiki slots, so `--shiki-light` and `--shiki-dark` hold the same value and the app's variant attribute is harmless; `auto` keeps the app's dual pair, which is why an app theme flip still recolours tokens with no re-tokenize. Verified in the browser: Dracula on a light app palette measured `rgb(40,42,54)` on `.cm-editor`, `rgb(248,248,242)` on `.cm-content`, the theme's own gutter ink, and identical `--shiki-light`/`--shiki-dark` per token; `Auto` returned the surface to transparent with `rgb(20,19,16)` ink; the choice survived a reload through `POST /api/settings`.
  **The module is fetched on SELECTION, never at boot** (`theme-loader.ts`, a `globalThis`-free per-process registry with in-flight dedupe). `codeToTokens` throws for a theme Shiki has not been given, so the decoration plugin subscribes to `onEditorThemeReady` and rebuilds once it lands — without that a freshly picked theme would leave the surface uncoloured until something else forced a rebuild. A theme that fails to load leaves the app palette, which is a wrong palette rather than a blank editor.
  **The setting reaches an already-open editor through an EVENT, not a prop** — `omp:editor-theme-changed`, dispatched by the modal only when the value actually moved, the same shape the editor FONT uses, because `appSettings` is a boot-time prop. `useEditorTheme()` returns `{theme, vars, themes}` in one hook rather than three subscriptions that could disagree for a frame. The two diff surfaces go through `ThemedMergeDiffView` (the panel's own file is at the size ceiling, and the chat's diff card is the same surface), so both wear the theme with no second subscription to keep in step.
  **Both editors drop `bg-paper` from the surface's own class list** — an explicit theme's background is opaque, so the panel colour under it would be a rectangle of the wrong shade at the edges; `auto` still shows the panel through `background-color: transparent`.
  **`Auto` is a CARD, not a checkbox**, and it is drawn under every light/dark filter — it is neither variant, and a user who filtered to Dark must still be able to get back to the app palette without first clearing the filter.
- **`SettingsState` holds only settings that do something.** 16 of its 26 fields were dead — declared and defaulted in the modal, never rendered, never read (`editorFont` among them, along with `tunnelEnabled`, `activeProvider`, `binaryPath`, …). They arrived in the Remix migration and were never implemented, and because `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` is only merged for display — `diffSettings` persists just the keys an edit moved — their fake defaults never reached SQLite either, so nothing downstream could have depended on them. `editorFont` was wired to the picker above rather than deleted; the other 15 were removed, and a field that only ever appears in `types/settings/state.ts` and `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` is now the signal that it was never wired.
- **The editor column has two WIDTH modes beside its full SCREEN one, and the three glyphs are three different shapes** (`shared/lib/workspace/editor-view-mode.ts` is the table, `client/hooks/workspace/editor-view.ts` reads it, `common/editor-view-buttons/` draws it). **Compact view** collapses the CHAT and gives its width to the editor; **Full view** collapses the chat AND the session sidebar; neither touches the right panel, because the outline, the search hits and the diff list are what a wider document is read against.
