The existing `fixed inset-0` control is a third, different thing — it covers the right panel too — so it keeps `Maximize` while compact draws a crossed-out message and full a closing panel edge: two spellings of one glyph is how a user learns to click the wrong one. **The mode is SHARED state, not a private copy per toolbar** (`useSharedSessionState`): the editor and the diff toolbar are the same surface on two tab kinds, so a mode entered from one must be the mode the other shows — a private copy left the diff reading `normal` after the editor entered compact. **The chat stays MOUNTED and is sized to zero** (`Panel` gained a collapsed-filler branch; the box is `inert`), because it owns the live run, the composer draft and the scroll position, and widening a document must not discard any of them. **The mode only means anything while the editor column is open**, and that is DERIVED rather than written back (`editorOpen` in the hook), so closing the column restores the chat and the sidebar and reopening returns to the width the user chose. **The layout math has one option for it** (`chatHidden`, `panel-widths.ts`): the chat floor and the pair's 70% share both exist to leave the conversation room, so with no conversation on screen the editor takes everything the right panel and the separators do not — its remembered fraction is left untouched, so leaving the mode restores it, and a drag made in the mode persists the RIGHT panel only (`readPanelWidths` skips the editor, whose width there is arithmetic rather than a choice). The sidebar toggle and `⌘B` route through `handleToggleLeftPanel`, which steps `full` back to `compact` — flipping the raw `showLeftPanel` flag would change a value the layout is ignoring and the click would do nothing. **Both toolbars are ONE row, and what does not fit goes into a `⋯` menu** (`common/toolbar-row/`, the rule in `shared/lib/ui/toolbar-overflow.ts`). Wrapping was the first answer and it is the wrong one: the editor panel is 320px wide on a 1440px screen, ten-plus controls do not fit, and a wrapped strip made the bar **72px tall** — the document moved down by a whole row and the second row read as a second toolbar. A horizontal scroll is worse (the diff toolbar already measured 361px of content in a 296px box with `Stage` entirely outside it and no scrollbar to say so). So the row is `flex-nowrap`, widths are MEASURED once per cluster set (a table of guessed pixels drifts with a label, an icon or a font), and the tail becomes menu rows NAMED IN WORDS. **Pinning is not the visual order**: Save is drawn first because that is where a reader looks, while the width modes sit last and are the reason the buttons exist — so `pinned` marks what a narrow panel must not bury (Save, the width modes and full screen on the editor; the change counts, the width modes, Stage and Edit File on the diff), and everything else is menu material. An unmeasured row draws everything (hiding before the width is known flashes a menu on every mount), and the pinned set is placed FIRST so an early unpinned control cannot consume the space a pinned one needs. The editor toolbar fits its row at the 320px floor with no trigger at all; the diff toolbar overflows there and shows `⋯` — both measured in the browser. Pinned by `editor-view-mode.test.ts` (the hide table and the toggle), `toolbar-overflow.test.ts` (the pinned rule), `toolbar-row/index.test.tsx` (a wide row draws everything, a narrow one keeps the pinned cluster and offers the rest as menu rows that run the same actions), `editor-view-buttons/index.test.tsx` (two toolbars mounted under one provider — they share the slot, each reports its own `aria-pressed`, the active mode returns to `normal`, a stored mode is restored) and `editor-views.e2e.ts` (real bounding boxes: compact zeroes the chat and keeps both other columns, full also zeroes the sidebar, the mode survives a reload, and the diff toolbar's buttons drive the same mode).
- **Editor & Diff toolbars**: every action is an icon-only `<button>` carrying both a `title` and an `aria-label` (a bare `<svg onClick>` is unreachable by keyboard), and each reflects its own state — word wrap / preview / full screen are `aria-pressed`, Save shows `saving` → `saved` → `error`, and Copy flashes a check for 1.5s. Two rules the toolbars exist to enforce: **a failed read is reported, never substituted** (`useFileEditor` has no `fallback`; the mock sample it used to drop in was written straight over the real file by the next save), and **a failed save says so** (`reportSaveError`, since a silent failure left the user believing the file was written). A file whose read failed also has Save disabled — its buffer is empty, so writing it would blank the file on disk. The save status is reset when the active tab changes, because it describes the file that was written. Both toolbars are `@container`s, because the panel — not the viewport — is what runs out of room: the editor panel is 320px wide on a 1440px screen. The diff toolbar's action strip therefore **wraps** instead of scrolling: `overflow-x-auto no-scrollbar` clipped it invisibly (measured 361px of content in a 296px box, with `Stage` entirely outside it and no scrollbar to say so), and it now costs one extra row on a narrow panel and nothing on a wide one. The editor toolbar's path block is hidden below `@[420px]` for the same reason — the action strip is 273px of `flex-shrink-0`, which left the path a measured 23px of truncated fragment (the tab above already carries the name) — and its action strip carries `ml-auto` so it stays right-aligned once that block is gone.
- **The diff panel and the chat's diff card render CodeMirror's merge view, not a table.** `diff-panel/merge.ts` owns the two shapes (`unifiedMergeView` for Unified, `MergeView` for Split) and `MergeDiffView.tsx` mounts one per shape; `diff-panel/{UnifiedView,SplitView}.tsx` and `shared/lib/fs/diff-parser.ts` are deleted. **The panel compares DOCUMENTS now**, so `oldContent`/`newContent` travel on the existing `GET /api/fs/git?fileDiff=1` response and *Diff Only* ↔ *Full Code* is a client-side `collapseUnchanged` COMPARTMENT reconfigure (a re-mount would drop the reader's scroll) — the server's `-U<n>` probe existed only because a table renderer cannot collapse anything, and `fullContext` is gone. **A changed token carries NO underline, and suppressing it is not a theme rule.** `@codemirror/merge` marks the changed characters with `.cm-changedText` (a deleted chunk's with `.cm-deletedText`) and its own BASE THEME paints a 2px gradient along the bottom of each fragment. A word-level change is usually several fragments — `2` → `22` is one — so the reader saw a run of short coloured bars under the code that reads as a second, broken baseline rather than as emphasis; the row's band and the gutter marker are the signals that remain, and `diff-panel.e2e.ts` asserts both directions (the bar gone, the band still painted). Three facts about where the suppression can live, each measured. (1) It CANNOT go in the panel's `EditorView.theme`: that rejects the `&light`/`&dark` selectors the package's rules are spelled with (`RangeError: Unsupported selector: &light`), and the throw took the whole merge view down leaving an empty host. (2) A plain `.cm-changedText { background: none }` LOSES — the package's rule is `.ͼ2.cm-merge-b .cm-changedText`, i.e. (0,3,0), and CodeMirror mounts its style modules AFTER `tailwind.css`. (3) So `tailwind.css` owns it with three class-level parts of its own (`.cm-editor` + `.cm-merge-a`/`.cm-merge-b` + the span class), winning on specificity rather than by `!important` — which would also beat a future theme that legitimately wants a different treatment. Verified in both dev and the production bundle (the emitted rule reads `background:0 0`). **The `+n/-n` counts come from `Chunk.build(a, b)`, never from `diff()`**: `diff()` is a CHARACTER diff, so changing `line 5` into `line 5 changed` reports one insertion of `" changed"` and no deletion, and a badge built on it claimed `+2` for two edited lines (measured: `Chunk.build` gives `+2 -2` for that pair, `+2 -0` for a new file, `+1 -0` for an inserted line). The chat card has a PATCH and no documents, so `shared/lib/fs/patch-sides.ts` rebuilds the two sides from the hunks — **padding each side to the line its `@@` header names**, or a mid-file change would be numbered from 1 — and collapses that padding with `contentMode: 'diff'`. The merge package's own colours are hardcoded hex; `diffTheme()` overrides them with the palette variables, or a diff would be the one surface that ignores the theme. **A card's diff SCROLLS because the CodeMirror root fills its host by LAYOUT, not by a percentage.** `.cm-editor`/`.cm-mergeView` carry `height: 100%`, and a percentage resolves against a DEFINITE containing block — the diff panel's host has one, but a chat card's diff is bounded only by `max-h-72`, and a box whose height comes from `max-height` is indefinite for that purpose. The root therefore computed to `auto` and grew to its content: measured on a real transcript, **1847px inside a 288px host**, clipped by `overflow-hidden` with `scrollHeight === clientHeight`, so a 30-line edit card showed nine rows with no way to reach the rest — in BOTH shapes (unified 1847, split 1928). `min-h-0` on the wrapper is necessary but NOT sufficient (it lets the item shrink; what the root needs is a height to shrink TO), and `DiffView.test.ts` used to claim it was. `[data-code-surface]` in `tailwind.css` is the other half: the host is a column flex container and the root a shrinking item (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0`), so the height arrives through layout whatever the host's own is — definite, capped, or automatic (an unconstrained `max-h-none` host still sizes to its content). The rule keys on the shared marker for that reason: both surfaces that draw a code surface restated this wrong once. `css.test.ts` gates the built rule and `tests/e2e/specs/tool-diff-scroll.e2e.ts` is the rendered proof (host 288 / root 288 / range > 200 / a scroll that moves, in both shapes; A/B'd — both cases fail with the rule removed), driven by the `edit:<pairs>` prompt directive the fake child gained (every row a CHANGE, or `contentMode: 'diff'` collapses the patch back under the ceiling and the range is 0).
- **Diff Panel toolbar**: `Discard` posts `actionType: 'revert'` — the server's own name for "restore this file from the index", and the one that handles the untracked case (removes the file). Sending `discard`, which no handler matched, returned `{success: true}` for doing nothing. Stage/unstage/discard await the response and surface a rejection in an inline error strip; the previous fire-and-forget submit flipped the badge optimistically and let a rejected action look successful. The Split/Unified segments each **set** their mode (toggling meant clicking the already-active segment flipped the view). The toolbar is an `@container`, so its labels collapse on a **narrow panel** rather than a narrow viewport — the editor panel is 320px wide on a 1440px screen, and `sm:` kept every label on, overflowing it. The `+n/-n` counts sit in the action strip rather than the identity block: the editor tab carries the file name but never the counts, and they are computed by `countChanges` from `Chunk.build` — the rows the merge view actually draws, not git's own tally (see the bullet above). A second segment pair beside Split/Unified chooses **how much of the file** is shown — `Diff Only` (default) or `Full Code`. It is a separate group because the two axes are orthogonal (Unified/Split is the shape of the rows, Full/Diff is how many there are), and each segment sets its own mode for the same reason the view pair does. The mode is a `collapseUnchanged` COMPARTMENT, so it reconfigures the live editor instead of re-mounting it — the documents are already both sides of the file, and the reader's scroll survives the toggle. `diff.contentMode` persists per session beside `diff.viewMode`. **Word wrap is a third toolbar toggle, on by default** (`diff.wordWrap`) — a long line that runs off a 320px panel hides the very change the diff was opened for. It is `EditorView.lineWrapping` in a compartment (measured: `scrollWidth` equals `clientWidth` wrapped, 4 525 against 320 unwrapped on a 600-char line), which is also why the old table-layout reasoning is gone with the tables.
- **Diff→editor conversion** goes through `useFileTabs().convertDiffToEditor`, which replaces the tab immutably and reuses `fileTabId` (`src/shared/lib/workspace/file-tab-id.ts`). The panel used to assign `activeFile.isDiff = false` on the object inside `layout.openedFiles`: React saw no change (identical array identity), and because that array is what gets persisted the mutation reached the database — the "diff" tab came back as a file tab after a reload and its diff never returned. If the target file already has a tab the diff entry is dropped instead, or the two would share an id.
- **`handleOpenFile` activates the id of the tab it MATCHED, never the id it computed.** The same path reaches the editor under two ids: the explorer passes a row's own `id` (its relative path, so `"notes.md"`), while a search row, a chat link and a drop compute `fileTabId(path)` (a hash). The handler computed `file.id ?? fileTabId(file.path)` and then activated that — after the updater had already found the existing tab under the OTHER id. The active id named no tab, so `Editor` found no active file and drew "Select a tab to view content" over the file that was already open, with the tab strip still showing it (measured in the browser: `layout.openedFiles[0].id = "notes.md"` against `layout.activeFileId = 1582000100`). The resolution happens OUTSIDE the updater, against `getSessionValue(...'layout.openedFiles')`: a state updater may run more than once, and activating a tab is not idempotent. Reading the store rather than the hook's own value keeps the callback's identity stable, which matters because the search panel's memoized rows depend on it. Pinned by `file-tabs.test.ts` (all three directions: existing tab keeps its id, a new file gets the computed one, and the reverse order dedupes) and `tests/e2e/specs/search-panel.e2e.ts` (a real click on a hit for a file the explorer opened).
- **Right Panel + Activity Bar**: switchable developer panels — `context` (Context & Telemetry), `files`, `search`, `git` (Source Control), `wiki`, `terminal` (Bun), `user-browser`, `browser`, `usage`, `todo` and `plan` (`src/client/components/workspace/`, `src/client/components/layout/RightActivityBar.tsx`).
- **The Files panel lists EVERY entry — there is no noise table.** `listDirectoryEntries` (`src/server/lib/fs/listing.ts`, shared by `/api/fs/dir` and the realtime `fs:` topic so the two cannot disagree) used to drop a hardcoded set of names (`node_modules`, `.git`, `dist`, `build`) at every depth. That hid directories the user had no other way to reach: `packages/android/dist` holds the built APKs, and the reported symptom was the folder simply not existing in the panel. A name cannot tell a dependency tree from a folder of release artifacts, so the panel's only signal is now the `ignored` flag — one `git check-ignore` per listing (never one per entry), which DIMS a git-ignored row instead of deleting it. Cost is bounded by laziness, not by filtering: children are read one directory per request and only when expanded (measured on this repo: root listing 59 ms, `node_modules` 50 ms, `.git` 21 ms), and a row nobody expands is never read. A folder holding exactly ONE folder and nothing else is collapsed into a single row naming the chain (`com/java/app/package`), because a row per level spends four rows to reach the files inside and every editor folds that. `folderChain` walks it in the server listing — one `readdir` per folder entry, and only for the folders that have exactly one entry (measured 0.77 ms for the 18 folders at this repository's root) — while the chain's own levels are never listed, so the read the user would have paid on each expand is one that no longer happens. The row's identity is the DEEPEST directory (`path`/`id`), which is what an expand reads and what a rename, delete, drag or paste acts on; only its `name` carries the chain. The walk stops at the first level that holds a file, two entries or nothing, so a folder of files never folds. The client folds by the same rule in `foldSingleChildFolders` (`shared/lib/fs/folder-chain.ts`, applied by `rehydrateTree` in `file-tree.ts` — the one function that builds a rendered tree, so every read, every topic snapshot and every local edit goes through it) because a create or a rename inserts a chain the server has not listed yet; `placeEntry` descends into a chain row by its own path, and when an entry lands BESIDE one it UNFOLDS that row rather than drawing the folder twice. The reveal after a create or rename therefore expands `drawnAncestors` — the rows that exist — never the path's own segments, which would put `lib` and `lib/deep` in the expansion set when the single row is `lib/deep`, and the next refresh would read each of them for nothing. A collapsed row is asked about its deepest path in the one `git check-ignore` call, which is enough: a rule naming an outer level answers for the deeper one too (verified). A rename or a delete acts on that deepest path, so the delete dialog names it explicitly (`this deletes <path> only`) rather than letting the chain label promise four directories and perform one. Pinned by `listing.test.ts`, `folder-chain.test.ts`, `file-tree.test.ts`, the panel's `index.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/specs/files-nested.e2e.ts` (a real chain on a real disk, expanded, and a rename beside it that unfolds it). The remaining name table, `SKIP_DIRS` in `routes/fs/read.ts`, belongs to the FLAT recursive walk behind `/api/fs/list` (the composer's `@` picker): that one is capped at `MAX_FILES` 3000, and descending into `node_modules` would spend the whole budget inside dependencies and leave the picker with no project file in it.
- **Every right-panel view is a PLUGIN, including the eleven Kabmo ships.** One catalog (`usePanelCatalog`, `src/client/components/workspace/plugin-panel/resolve.tsx`) merges the built-in views and the installed plugins, and the activity bar, the desktop panel stack and the phone's tab strip all render from it — so a view cannot be a table in one layout and a fetch in the other. `RIGHT_PANEL_TYPES` (`src/shared/lib/workspace/right-panels.ts`) stays the single source of the ORDER (it is shared, with no client import, because the width map and the persisted-id guard read it too), and `builtin.tsx` (`src/client/components/workspace/panels/`) is the built-in half: id, title, label, icon, a `lazy()` component, sizing and `requiresWorkspace`. **The `lazy()` wrapper is created at MODULE scope**, which is what `lazy-panels.tsx` used to arrange: a view's code stays in its own chunk, and because the wrapper's identity is stable across renders a view stays MOUNTED (its tree expansion, search results and terminal buffer alive) while the layout hides it with CSS. `requiresWorkspace` and `liveWhileHidden` are DATA rather than conditions at a call site: a view that reads the working tree is inert without a folder while one that reads the SESSION (`todo`, `plan`, `usage`, both browsers) is not — gating those showed "No session selected" over a list that was right there — and only the terminal stays live while another view is on screen, because it owns a live PTY. `panel-meta.tsx` and `lazy-panels.tsx` are gone with it; there is no per-view branch left in any layout.
- **Enablement is ONE axis and one store, and the ID PREFIX is what keeps the two kinds apart.** A built-in view is switched off by its BARE id (`git`, `files`, `terminal`) and a plugin by `plugin:<pluginId>` — the same key the client filters its catalog by and the same key the server tests before publishing a bundle. That prefix is load-bearing on the server too: `registry.server.ts` gates a plugin's bundle on `pluginPanelKey(manifest.id)`, and testing the bare manifest id there made a disabled plugin still report `enabled: true` while its panel stayed hidden — two answers for one question. The server stores whatever ids it is given and never learns which are built-in; `disabledPanels` travels on `GET /api/panels` and the client resolves its own views against it. **Absent means enabled**, which is what makes a freshly installed plugin live immediately, what shows every built-in view on a fresh install, and what keeps a database written before this feature from switching everything off. Two surfaces write the same set through the same `setEnabled(id, enabled)`: the activity bar's right-click menu (every panel, on or off, with its row kept while off — the only place it can be switched back on; nothing is pinned any more) and Settings → Panel Plugins' `BuiltinList` (all eleven with the switch a plugin card gets, and no Remove, because the code is in the bundle). The retired `hiddenRightPanels` list is folded into the disabled set ONCE, guarded by the old key's PRESENCE and deleting it as it goes, so the pass is idempotent and a view switched back on afterwards cannot be re-adopted (`migrateHiddenPanels`).
- **Two browser panels, one toolbar, and only one of them may offer device presets.** `browser-panel/` mirrors the tab this session's omp agent drives in the project-shared Chromium (a read-only screencast: status, URL, zoom, include-in-chat, copy, reconnect); `user-browser-panel/` is the user's own private iframe with real history and navigation. Both render `browser-toolbar/`, whose device cluster (`BrowserViewportSelector` + `ViewportMode`) is **optional** — the user panel passes it, the agent panel deliberately does not. A preset is genuine emulation only where the CSS box *is* the layout viewport (an iframe); on the agent panel it could just letterbox a fixed-resolution JPEG while claiming to emulate a device, and the cluster's 401px pushed the bar to `scrollWidth 482 > clientWidth 373` on a 373px panel, crushing the read-only URL to 18px. Agent-side emulation belongs to the agent itself (`tab.emulate()` / `browser.open({ viewport })`). `VIEWPORT_CLASSES` (`common/viewport.ts`) is therefore user-panel-only.

### Message-Footer Actions (Retry / Copy / Undo / New-chat)
- **A run footer describes the run, but its actions act on the TURN.** `RunFooter` gets the run's owner row (its last real AI message) for model/usage/duration, and `resolveRunFooters` now also carries the run's own boundary: `runStartIndex` / `runUserId` (the user row that opened it, `-1`/`''` for a transcript that starts mid-run) and `answerText` (every non-notice row between them, joined). An omp run spans thinking → tool calls → answer, so the owner row is almost never the row a user turn sits in front of. Measured on 498 real sessions (1453 footers): **Retry fired on 19.7% of footers before and 99.2% after**, and the copy payload differs from the owner row's own text on **48.4%** of runs (127 runs where the old copy produced nothing at all).
- **Retry re-runs the run's opening user row, and rewinds the JSONL first.** The old lookup required the owner's immediate predecessor to be a user turn and returned the list unchanged otherwise — a silent no-op that still aborted the live run, so clicking mid-stream stopped the answer and did nothing else. A real omp session now posts `POST /api/chat/:id/rewind` for that user row before re-sending: dropping only the client's rows left the abandoned turn on disk, and the next reload brought the "retried" answer back beside its replacement. The row-before-the-owner fallback survives for a caller that still hands over an AI row id.
- **Undo and Retry live together in `src/client/hooks/chat/timeline/rewind-actions.ts`** because they share those rules plus one more: **a refusal is reported**. `requestRewind` (`client/hooks/chat/timeline/rewind.ts`) returns the server's own reason, `reportActionError` (wired to the chat's toast stack from `ChatTimeline`) surfaces it, and the Undo dialog keeps itself open with an inline line as well. Previously a 400 was a bare `false` that only stopped the modal from closing — indistinguishable from a slow request.
- **A rewind cut point is a turn, not just a user message — and not always a record IN the file.** `isRewindCutPoint` (`server/lib/omp/session/rewind-file.ts`) accepts `type:"message"` with `message.role:"user"` **and** `type:"custom_message"` with `customType:"skill-prompt"` — omp expands `/skill:<name>` into a custom record and writes no user message for it, so the route's original role test refused every skill turn with `entry_not_found`. A builtin command (`/usage`, `/compact`, `/context`) goes further: it writes **no entry at all**, so its timeline row lives only in Kabmo overlay under a client-side id (`msg-…-user`). The client therefore sends that row's own `startedAt`, and `truncateSessionBody` places a cut the file cannot name at the first file turn at or after that clock (a clock past every turn cuts at the end). `overlayCutClock` is the **requested row's** clock in that case, not the file turn's, so undoing one `/usage` also removes the next one; the overlay prune (`pruneStoredAfterCut`) drops rows at or after it, plus the file turns the cut removed. Verified over every user row of all 498 sessions: 1452 cuts, **0 refusals, 0 resurrections on reload, 0 earlier rows lost**.
- **A failed Undo leaves the draft alone.** The composer text is restored only AFTER the rewind succeeds: restoring it first read as "undo worked" while the turn was still on screen, and had to be cleared by hand.
- **A failed Copy says so instead of doing nothing.** `CopyButton` disables itself on an empty payload with a reason in its title/aria-label, because 8.1% of runs genuinely have no text anywhere (tool-only or aborted turns) and the old `if (!text) return` swallowed the click. The run footer passes its own "Nothing to copy — this run produced no text" wording.
- **New-chat from a row carries the row's attachments, and seeds the draft as a QUOTATION.** `onNewChat(content, attachments)` seeds `NewChatModal`, so an image or inlined text file is not dropped from the branched chat. The draft itself goes through `seedNewChatDraft` (`shared/lib/chat/new-chat-seed.ts`): the row's text, a blank line, then `---`, so the user types their instruction UNDER the quoted message instead of into the same paragraph. Two properties of that placement are load-bearing. The blank line before `---` is what keeps it a thematic break — a `---` directly under a line of text is a setext H2 underline, and the timeline renders every user turn as markdown, so the seeded message would come back as a heading. And the divider sits AFTER the seed, never before: omp parses a command token at the START of the prompt, so prefixing would turn a leading `/usage` into prose and stop it running. **Do not "harden" this by fencing the seed** — a fence changes exactly one thing (a command stops being a command), while protecting nothing: nothing on Kabmo's side executes that text, and a `@agent` mention is rewritten by `translateAgentMentions` whether or not it sits in a fence (measured: raw, fenced and `---`-prefixed seeds all rewrite the mention). It would also turn the TUI-only names (`/plan`, `/clear`, `/hotkeys`) from a refused-and-reported turn into literal text the model improvises around, which is the failure `composer/tui-only.ts` exists to prevent.
- **The New Chat modal maximizes, and its composer grows with a ceiling.** A header button toggles the card between its default `42rem / 90vh` and an expanded `min(96vw, 1600px) / 92vh`, and the composer's textarea sizes itself to its content — `useAutoGrow` (`hooks/chat/composer/auto-grow.ts`) measures `scrollHeight` and clamps it between 80px and a ceiling (320px, 480px expanded), scrolling past it. The chat composer deliberately does NOT grow: its textarea is a fixed box, because the timeline owns that space and a composer growing on every paste would move the conversation under the reader. Two failure modes are pinned here. The card's size is applied as an **inline style, not `h-[92vh]`/`max-w-[min(96vw,1600px)]`** — an arbitrary-value class only exists if the CSS generator emitted a rule for that exact string, and a missing rule fails silently (the toggle reported `aria-pressed="true"` while the card kept its content height; measured in the browser, and the dev CSS bundle does not always regenerate). And the measurement resets `height` to `auto` first, because `scrollHeight` reports the height the box would need AT its current size — without the reset the box never shrinks back after a deletion.
- **The seeded bytes travel through `attachmentImage`, which also reads a `data:…;base64,` preview** — the load path never sets `dataBase64`, so a replayed image had no payload and was silently omitted from a retry or a seeded chat. `attachmentHasPayload` is the one predicate the composer's send guard uses, so a replayed image is not mistaken for an unreadable one.
- **Side effects that must happen EXACTLY once never sit inside a `setLocalMessages` updater.** Undo/Retry compute the truncated list from `localMessagesRef` and persist outside it; the updaters that still call `persistMessages` are full-array overwrites of the same row, which re-applying cannot double (noted at each site). `submitNewChat`'s `any[]` became `Attachment[]`, and committed rows are normalized through `toAttachmentList` rather than cast.
- **The pending "new-…" view offers the same actions as the real timeline.** `EmptyWorkspacePrompt` renders the same rows, so `ChatTimeline` passes `onUndo` / `onRetry` / `onNewChat` into it; without them the optimistic turn's footer and its user bubble drew buttons that did nothing until the omp session was adopted.

- **The New Chat dialog's first send is PARKED and fired by the render that carries its pending id — never dispatched from the handler that opened the dialog.** `useNewChatSubmit` (`hooks/chat/timeline/new-chat.ts`) writes `new-…` into the URL, parks `{ pendingId, text, attachments }` and lets an effect call the send path once `sessionId === pendingId`. The previous shape (`setTimeout(() => executeSend(...), 0)` inside `submitNewChat`) captured the render the dialog was opened in, so the FIRST prompt left against the session the dialog was opened FROM — for an omp session it landed in the previous chat, no spawn ever happened, no sidebar spinner was armed (the run had no server row at all), and the new chat showed an optimistic bubble with no stream until the operator typed a second prompt, which worked because the composer's own handler reads the pending scope. The same call carries the workspace: the pending session gets a `folderId` (the current session's owning folder, else the composer's pick, never overwriting one already in the URL), because the first send SPAWNS in that folder's cwd and a dialog opened from a session picked in the sidebar had no `folderId` at all — the send would have fallen back to the simulated stream. Verified in the browser against an isolated MOCK instance: the send navigated to `?sessionId=new-…&folderId=3`, issued no POST to the session the dialog was opened from, and rendered exactly one user bubble; `new-chat.test.ts` fails with the old dispatch restored.

### Generating State (timeline & composer dock)
- **One "a run is in flight" state serves the whole timeline: `timelineRunning = isChatRunning(streamStatusMap, sessionId, ownRun)`** (`ChatTimeline`, `hooks/chat/timeline/index.ts`). `streamStatusMap` is the provider's `sessionId → streamStatus` map built from the `sidebar:status` topic (`hooks/chat/omp/session-list.tsx`), and `isSessionStreaming` (`hooks/chat/omp/session-statuses.ts`) is the exact `streamStatus === 'stream'` test the session item's `Loader2` spinner is drawn from. The docked indicator, the streaming row's marker and the withheld run footer all read `timelineRunning`, so they cannot disagree with the sidebar, and a run this page did not start — a second tab, a second Kabmo instance (the status row lives in the shared database and the child belongs to whichever process owns it), a scheduled task, a page that never attached to the stream because `GET /api/agent/:id` answers `running: false` for a wrapper this process does not hold — is still reported. **The server row cannot cover two windows, and BOTH must be reported or a run reads as already settled while it is still starting.** (1) A pending `new-…` chat has no omp id until the spawn answers, so no row can ever carry it. (2) A run THIS PAGE dispatched, in the beat between the send arming the optimistic rows and the `stream` row reaching this client over the topic — measured at ~120-250 ms, and the reason the run footer "appeared for an instant" on every prompt: with the row alone, `resolveRunFooters` was handed `isGenerating: false` for that window, settled the run, and painted a model/duration footer over the EMPTY placeholder row, then withdrew it the moment the row arrived. The second term is `ownRun` — the render-visible mirror of `ownRunRef`, armed by the send path alone (`setGenerating(true, true)`) and released by the same settle paths as the run — NOT the raw local flag: an ADOPTED run (the mount probe resuming a live run's UI) leaves it alone, so a run this page did not start is still reported by the server row and never by client state. The per-frame browser proof of that window (`chat.e2e.ts`) was removed: it sampled the DOM every animation frame and flaked on three runs, so it reported timing rather than the footer. **A THIRD source must not exist, and it is what this used to have.** `stream-pending` was a client-side mark armed by the send path and released by `onAgentEnd`/`onPromptSettled`, and its release depended on the client naming the session id it had armed: a mark armed for a pending `new-…` id and released under the adopted omp uuid was never released at all, and a mark re-armed by `onResumeStream` outlived the run it described. It forced `stream` into the sidebar's `folders`, so `isSessionStreaming` read it too and BOTH surfaces kept spinning over a finished turn — the reported "spinner and generating indicator run again after the stream has finished". The whole mechanism is gone (`stream-overlay.ts` → `sidebar-overlay.ts`, which now holds only the seen-strip and the title hint). The composer's own `isGenerating` prop stays the flag for Send-vs-queue and the model/thinking picks deferred to the next prompt — those belong to the run this client is driving — but **Stop follows `timelineRunning`**, because a run this page did not start (a second tab on the same session, a scheduled task, the goal driver's continuation, a reload that never re-attached) is still a run, the sidebar's spinner says so, and hiding Stop for it left the only control that ends a turn unavailable with nothing on screen to explain why. The press stays honest about ownership: `POST /api/agent/:id` answers a stop for a session this process does not hold with `session_not_running` (409) instead of falling through to the spawn path (`LIVE_RUN_COMMANDS`, `rpc/constants.ts`), which would resume the session FILE — a second writer on a file the owning instance is streaming — answer `abort` against the child it had just created, and then, on the `force_reset` escalation, destroy that same child. A run streaming on another instance therefore shows the rotating `COOL_VERBS` fallback: no frame ever named its activity here.
- **A run omp no longer owns is settled by Kabmo, on omp's own quiescence verdict** (`src/server/lib/omp/rpc/run-settle.server.ts`). A `stream` row is released by a terminal `agent_end`, and two shapes never send one: omp ends the turn with `agent_end {isTerminal:false}` (a detached subagent, a background job, a queued continuation) and the continuation never arrives, or the frame stream stops for any other reason. `streaming` then stays true for the life of the process, and nothing reaches the row: its owner is THIS process and it is alive, so the sidebar heal's staleness half sees a live pid and its orphan half only releases rows whose run this process does NOT hold; and `GET /api/agent/:id` answers a busy session from local flags precisely so a `get_state` cannot queue behind the running turn, so the one reconciler is never called. Measured on a real stranded child: the row survived 90s of sidebar loads and the attach probe answered `busy:true` throughout, while the transcript was complete — the reported "finished session spins forever". The repair is a probe of `get_state.isSettled` (omp's own "nothing live, admitted, scheduled, queued or awaiting background work"), and it is the ONLY thing allowed to settle: anything short of `true` touches no flag, so a mid-run probe can never blank a live run. The clock (`RunSettle`) is fed by every frame, fires after `RUN_SILENCE_MS` (30s) of quiet, and an announced retry wait (`auto_retry_start`'s `delayMs`) pushes the deadline out by the wait omp promised. On a settle it reconciles the flags through `buildWebState`, writes `finish` and emits the terminal `agent_end` — without that frame the clients keep `isGenerating` and the withheld run footer armed (verified end to end: row `stream`→`finish` at t+32s with no restart, and in the browser the indicator was replaced by the settled run footer). It is armed from the module (`loadSidebarData` asks each live wrapper, fire-and-forget, so the list never waits on a probe) and awaited from the attach probe, which is where the user's own click lands.
- **A provider retry is NAMED, because the run legitimately stays open across it.** omp replays transient provider errors by itself (`retry.enabled`, `retry.maxRetries`, honoring the provider's `retry-after`) and each retry announces itself with `auto_retry_start` and opens a fresh `agent_start` — no `agent_end` in between, so the sidebar spinner and the indicator were correct while the timeline said nothing about why nothing moved (measured: 2m43s on a real quota wall, last visible row an error). `shared/lib/chat/timeline/provider-retry.ts` folds those frames: the indicator reads `Retrying after a provider error 2/3 · next in 7s`, and the FIRST attempt of a saga (`attempt === 1`) leaves one persisted notice row so a reloaded chat still records it. **The phrase is sticky for the saga's duration** (`providerRetryVerbRef`, consulted by `setActivity`) because otherwise the attempt's own frames name a phase — `turn_start`/`agent_start` say "Thinking…" over a request that is about to fail, which is the uninformative spinner this replaced. Two traps are pinned by tests: the saga's own `agent_start` re-enters the loop and `onAgentStart` writes the indicator directly (bypassing the mirror), so the override must publish even when the mirror already holds the phrase; and a terminal `agent_end` closes the saga, since it can arrive with no `auto_retry_end` in front of it. Never settle a retrying run: `get_state.isSettled` is `false` throughout one (verified against real omp), which is what lets the two mechanisms share one predicate.
- **A committed history fetch never lands while a run THIS page started is live; the guard is keyed on `ownRunRef`, never on `isGeneratingRef` and never on the AI placeholder.** `useSessionLoad` (`hooks/chat/timeline/session-load.ts`) skips the `/api/chat/:id` payload entirely — no replace, no merge, no emptying — while a run THIS page dispatched is streaming, because that payload is a LAGGING snapshot: for a fresh spawn omp may not have written the session file at all (its writes are buffered until the first assistant message settles) and it never carries a segment still streaming. Keying the guard on the AI placeholder instead (`isGenerating && aiPlaceholderIdRef`) re-opened it mid-run, because the stream releases the placeholder at the first assistant `message_end` while the run continues — and both failure shapes were measured on real sessions: an empty payload emptied the list (the just-sent turn vanished), and a payload carrying the file's own copy of that turn was merged UNDER the live rows, whose ids differ (`msg-…-user` vs the echoed id), so the turn rendered twice. Keying it on `isGeneratingRef` alone was worse and is the bug this fixes: the mount probe that RESUMES a live run's generating UI (`useOmpAgent` → `onResumeStream`) sets that flag too, so merely OPENING (or reloading) a session another writer was streaming discarded its whole transcript — measured on a real 333-message run: 0 rows on screen with only a `Load earlier messages` button, whose click paged a window into an empty head and so appeared to do nothing. `ownRunRef` is claimed by the send path alone (`setGenerating(true, true)` in `hooks/chat/timeline/send.ts`) and released by `setGenerating(false)`, so a run merely ADOPTED still loads its history and an empty payload still clears a non-pending session. `session-load.test.ts` pins every direction (the own-run skip, the adopted-stream load, and the idle mount's load/clear).
- **The docked indicator names the WAIT when the run's identity is not known yet, instead of drawing an empty one.** A fresh spawn has no provider/model for its first seconds: a pending `new-…` session has no sidebar row yet, so `runModel` is null, and the resolved model only arrives with the spawn response (`seedSession`). `GeneratingIndicator` therefore renders `Loading…` when neither half resolves, rather than a bullet with nothing in front of it (`• Thinking…`, which is what the first prompt of a new session showed — measured in the browser: `Loading...` until the spawn answered, then `Kenari · Deepseek-V4-1-Flash · Thinking…`). One half is enough to name the run: a provider-only identity still says which account is answering. The rule lives in the SHARED component, so the chat dock, the empty-workspace view and the BTW panel cannot disagree about it.
- **The run footer is withheld from the streaming row by POSITION, never by its role spelling.** `resolveRunFooters` skips the footer while generating when `ownerIndex === streamingRowIndex(messages)`, and the guard must not also require the row's role to be `ai`: that is only the LIVE mapper's spelling, while `roleFor` (`shared/lib/omp/session/messages-map.ts` → `messages-parse.ts`) spells the rows a JSONL load produces for omp's `developer`, `custom` and `toolResult` entries `assistant` — and a run reopened mid-flight has exactly such a row at its tail, the last entry written before the streaming answer. Gating on `ai` settled the footer on the row the answer was still streaming into, which is what "the AI footer already rendered while the stream was running" was. `ownerIndex` only ever holds a non-user, non-notice row, so no role test is needed. Pinned by `run-footer.test.ts` and a rendered `MessageList.test.ts` mount (both fail with the role test restored), plus the `timelineRunning` gate above: a run this page did not start used to settle its footer too, for the same reason it hid the indicator.

### Markdown Tables (chat timeline, editor preview, wiki)
- **Every rendered table carries its own scroll container, because a table cannot be narrowed and the timeline scroller is `overflow-x-hidden`.** `width: 100%` on an auto-layout table is a FLOOR, not a ceiling — the used width is `max(100%, the sum of the columns' min-content widths)` — so one long unbreakable token (a path, a 64-char digest, a URL) makes the table wider than the message box it sits in, and the scroller's `overflow-x-hidden` then clipped its right columns with no way to reach them. Measured inside the real timeline at 1440: a 4-column table came out **1444px wide in a 776px scroller, its right edge 696px past the scroller's right edge** — desktop and phone alike. The `table` renderer in `src/shared/lib/markdown/marked.ts` now wraps the BASE renderer's own output in `<div class="md-table-scroll">…</div>` (delegating to `Renderer.prototype.table` rather than rebuilding `thead`/`tbody`, so aligned cells and escaping stay marked's business), and `.prose-content .md-table-scroll` (`src/client/tailwind.css`) is that scroll container: `overflow-x: auto` carrying the block's vertical margin, with the table's own `margin` reset to 0 inside it — a scroll container is a BFC, so a child's margin cannot collapse out and would otherwise be drawn twice. The table's `width: 100%` is deliberately unchanged: a table that FITS keeps its full-width look (narrow table measured exactly the wrapper's width at both 1440 and 390), and only an over-wide one scrolls itself.
- **The scope is every markdown surface, not just agent output** — the same renderer serves the editor's file preview (`document`) and the wiki reader (`wikiScope`), and a table inside a user's own bubble is bounded by the bubble's `max-w-[92%]` clamp. Verified end to end in the browser, through the real pipeline (`renderMarkdown` → `sanitizeHtml` → the rendered row), on an isolated MOCK server: at 390px the sent user row's wrapper measured `clientWidth 307 / scrollWidth 1218` and a bubble-shaped probe built from `MessageItem`'s own classes `285 / 1142`, at 1440 the sent row's `598 / 1053` — and in every case `scrollLeft` reached the last column (911px of range on the phone, 455px on desktop) while the timeline scroller stayed at `scrollWidth === clientWidth` and `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` stayed at the viewport (`390`/`1440`). No bare `.prose-content > table` remains in the timeline, so nothing is left to be clipped. Cells also gained `overflow-wrap: break-word`, so ordinary prose wraps before a table ever needs to scroll.
- **`src/shared/lib/markdown/table.test.ts` pins the wrapper per table** — one wrapper per table (not one around a run of them), the table as its child, and — the load-bearing half — that it survives `sanitizeHtml` with its class intact, since a stripped `class` would restore the clipping silently. It covers agent output, `allowHtml` document previews and prose without a table. Note the harness trait its fixtures work around: happy-dom's sanitizer path unwraps the FIRST root element of a fragment (`<div>x</div>` → `x`; Chromium keeps it, verified with DOMPurify in a real browser), so fixtures lead with a paragraph the way a message does.

### Agent Event Stream Transport
- The live omp agent bridge (`POST /api/agent/:sessionId` for commands) streams events over **WebSocket by default** — `GET /api/agent/:sessionId/ws` — with **SSE** (`/api/agent/:sessionId/events`) as the fallback, selected in **Settings → Chats → Streaming Transport** (`streamTransport` in `kabmo_settings`; default `websocket`).
- Server side is one Elysia app for dev and prod: `src/server/index.ts` owns the port and mounts `src/server/routes/`, so the agent WebSocket (`src/server/routes/agent/ws.ts`) is a first-class `.ws()` route on that same listener. Started by `bun run dev` (watched) and `bun run start` / `kabmo serve` (production by default; `--dev` runs the source entry).
- Client side: `src/shared/lib/chat/omp/{transport,socket,sse}.ts` own the connections; both hand every frame to `src/shared/lib/chat/omp/agent-events.ts`, which folds it into Kabmo state. Keep frame handling in that folder — never fork behavior per transport. The two connectors are transport-generic (`connectSocket`/`connectEvents` take a URL); a session's BTW stream dials the same pair against `/api/btw/:sessionId/*` instead of adding a third flavour.
- **A spawned child streams `message_update` in FULL mode, because delta mode drops the tool call's identity.** `configureChild` (`src/server/lib/omp/rpc/child-config.ts`, called from `manager.ts` `initialize`) sends `set_event_filter {events:null, messageUpdates:"full"}`, so each frame carries the whole accumulated message and the client folds it as-is. `"delta"` was measured ~101× smaller on the wire (108 KB / 486 frames against 10.9 MB / 2217 on a 300-word prompt), but it drops the name and id a streaming tool call needs: `toolcall_delta` carries ONLY `{contentIndex, delta}` and `toolcall_start` carries no block at all, so both live solely on the `message_start` seed — which does NOT reliably contain the block. Measured on omp 18.10.3, the same model produced a seed of `[text, toolCall]` on one run and `[thinking]` on the next, so a card rendered a bare **Tool** for the whole argument stream and only snapped to `Write — <path>` at `toolcall_end`, which also swapped the card id (`tool-1` → `call_…`) and remounted it, dropping the reader's expand state. `full` makes the name and id readable from the first delta; the terminal frames (`message_start`, `message_end`, `turn_end`, `agent_end`) carry the full message either way. `delta-accumulator.ts` and `partial-json.ts` (the rebuild the delta projection required) are gone with it.
- **The frames that are NOT about the conversation live in `transport-frames.ts`.** `rpc_frame_error` (omp dropped a frame that exceeded the transport limit — names the type, so the notice says which step is missing), `extension_error` (an extension threw; omp keeps the session alive, so this is the only place it surfaces) and `session_settled` (the session went quiet — releases the optimistic generating state without waiting for the sidebar's next poll) are folded there, and so are the frames deliberately ignored (`cache_warming_*`, `retry_fallback_*`, `config_warnings_changed`, `ttsr_triggered`, `todo_reminder`, `todo_auto_clear`, `irc_message`) — listing them makes the omission a decision. `agent-events.ts` delegates to it from its `default:` branch.
- **A topic's `attach` is bound once per SUBSCRIPTION, so a replaced SOURCE needs an explicit `rebind`.** `hub.subscribe` runs the descriptor's `attach` when the first subscriber arrives and its cleanup when the last leaves — the topic is wired to whatever the source was at that moment. A session's omp child is routinely REPLACED while a tab stays subscribed: the idle reaper reclaims it after 10 quiet minutes (`IDLE_REAP_MS`), a composer access-mode change respawns it (`reconcileSpawnApprovalMode`), a crash destroys it, and `force_reset` (Stop's escalation) tears it down. Without a rebind the topic keeps pointing at the DEAD child's fanout: the subscriber holds its subscription and receives the fresh snapshot (so `onResumeStream` resumes the generating indicator) but **no frames ever arrive** — the answer looks like it never streamed until a reload re-subscribes and re-attaches. That is the reported "second prompt sometimes doesn't stream until I reload", and the spawn path is what fixes it: `startRpcSession`/`startNewRpcSession` raise the `session-attached` signal the moment the new child is reachable, and `publishSessionState` (`topics.server.ts`) now calls `getRealtimeHub().rebind(topic)` before its `republishSnapshot`. `rebind` drops the stale cleanup, re-runs `attach`, and is a no-op with no subscribers (a topic nobody watches must not hold a source open). Pinned by `hub.server.test.ts` (frames follow the replaced source; no subscribers → no attach) and `topics.server.test.ts` (a `session-attached` signal detaches once, re-attaches, and re-snapshots the subscriber).
- **Steering uses omp's `steer`, never `abort_and_prompt`.** "Send Now (Steering)" on a queued row used to post `abort_and_prompt`, which CANCELS the running turn and starts a new one — the answer the user was watching was thrown away. Measured on omp 18.8.3 with a counting turn: `steer` produced one `agent_start`/`agent_end` pair with the model answering inside the same run, `abort_and_prompt` produced two of each. `OmpAgentHandle.steerOmpRun` (`src/client/hooks/chat/omp/index.ts`) posts `steer`, and because a steer creates no run boundary the `interruptPendingRef` guard that existed to survive `abort_and_prompt`'s abort-then-restart is gone entirely.
- **The run's tool bookkeeping survives an arrival that is the SAME conversation, and the STREAM is what proves it.** `useOmpAgent` clears `toolResultsRef` / `lastToolMessageRef` / the activity phrase when the session on screen is a different conversation, because they belong to one run: emptied mid-turn, every later `tool_execution_update` / `tool_execution_end` has no target to pair onto and the card freezes at `Running` for the rest of the session (measured in `wait.e2e.ts`: the settle spec read `Wait Running` for its whole 30s budget, and the browser trace showed `refresh … last null` between two progress frames of one call). `isRunSwitch` (`client/hooks/chat/omp/run-switch.ts`) is the rule, and three arrivals are NOT switches: a spawn adoption (`new-…` → UUID, answered by `isSpawnAdoption` without claiming the record the session-state provider owns), the same id again (a re-render, a metadata refresh), and **a send that began with no `?sessionId=` at all** — a chat opened from a workspace, where the URL carries only `?folderId=`. That third case is why the pending-id record is not enough on its own: no pending id ever existed, and the spawn path connects the stream BEFORE it writes the URL, so `attachedSession() === sessionId` is the evidence. A session merely opened from the sidebar satisfies none of them — nothing dials it until the mount probe runs, which is after this decision — so it still clears and still probes for a live run. Pinned by `run-switch.test.ts`.
- **The channel never gives up, because a phone freezes the tab.** A backgrounded Android tab (Brave/Chrome) stops running timers, and the server's 30s protocol heartbeat (`attachObserverStream`, `STREAM_HEARTBEAT_MS`) tears the socket down because a frozen tab cannot answer a ping. Coming back, the client's backoff spent a 4-dial budget in ~7.5s — often before the radio woke from Doze — and then latched `error`: `scheduleReconnect` returned with no timer and `connectIfNeeded` refused to dial from `error`, so nothing could ever recover and only a reload repaired the stream (the reported "indicator goes red and the stream stops until I reload"). Three rules replace that, all in `src/shared/lib/realtime/{connection,state}.ts` (split so each file stays under the size ceiling: `state.ts` owns the socket slot, the status and the frame sink; `connection.ts` owns dial, backoff, the resume probe and teardown). **The fast budget is a cadence, not a give-up** — past `MAX_FAILED_DIALS` the channel keeps retrying at `SLOW_RETRY_MS` (30s), so a tab that never sees a resume event still repairs itself. **A resume event resets the budget and dials at once** — `visibilitychange` (only when `!document.hidden`), `pageshow` (a bfcache restore) and `online` (the radio can return before the page is visible), installed once per state object so a `bun --hot` re-evaluation cannot double them. **A socket the browser still reports OPEN is PROBED, never trusted**: a frozen tab never learns the server hung up, so `wake()` sends an application-level `{t:'ping'}` and waits `WAKE_PROBE_MS` (2s) for the server's `pong` (`notePong`, called from the topic layer's already-decoded `pong` case) — an unanswered probe closes that socket and the normal retry path takes over. Two guards are load-bearing and each was a bug first: `socket.onclose` returns early when `host.socket !== socket`, or the abandoned socket's late `close` nulls the LIVE dial and schedules a third; and `dial()` returns when nothing is wanted, or a retry scheduled before the last subscriber left opens a socket nobody reads. `RealtimeStatus` lost `error` entirely — a state meaning "stopped trying" has no way back into `connecting` — so a dead channel reads `closed` (red) while a retry is pending. **And a phone's WebView delivers NONE of those events, which is why a WATCHDOG exists** (`resume.ts` + `watchdog.ts`, split so each stays under the ceiling: `watchdog.ts` is the pure decision, `resume.ts` the driver that owns the interval, the listeners and the probe). Measured on the shipped Android shell: a backgrounded WebView is never reported hidden, so returning to the app fires no `visibilitychange`, and a socket the server reaped while the page was frozen produces no `close` — `readyState` still says OPEN. The channel therefore sat on a corpse with `status === 'open'`, no frames, the answer frozen mid-sentence, and only a reload repaired it (the reported "websocket tidak terhubung, stream tidak lanjut, harus reload"). A running interval is the one signal a frozen page cannot fake, because a frozen page stops running it: `LIVENESS_TICK_MS` (5s) reads two clocks and asks `watchdogAction` — a tick arriving later than `FREEZE_DETECT_MS` means the page was SUSPENDED (treated as a resume: reset the budget, probe or re-dial), and an OPEN socket silent past `IDLE_PROBE_AFTER_MS` (25s, below the server's 30s reap so the question is asked BEFORE the reap) is probed even when nothing woke the page, which also catches a connection that died on a Wi-Fi→cellular handoff while the tab stayed visible. Freeze detection is gated on `!document.hidden` because a hidden tab is legitimately throttled to about a tick a minute and every one of those would read as a freeze; the idle probe is NOT gated, so a connection that died while the user was in another app is repaired before they return. Two further guards, each a bug first: a probe armed before a freeze keeps its deadline timer frozen with it, so `probeId` stays set forever and every later probe returns early — `probeArmedAt` is what lets an arm that outlived its own deadline be discarded; and the interval is stopped with the last subscription and on `resetConnection`, or a released channel keeps probing nothing. **The shell says so directly as well**: `window.__kabmoResume()` (`client/lib/native/resume.ts`, installed in `main.tsx` like `__kabmoBack`) is evaluated from `MainActivity.onResume` / `ShellViewController.viewDidAppear`, so the repair starts on that tick rather than at the next watchdog one — inert in a browser, and a no-op with nothing subscribed, which is why the shell may call it on every foreground. Pinned by `watchdog.test.ts` (the decision table, no clock needed), `watchdog-wiring.test.ts` (the interval reaching the probe and re-dial paths, and the stale arm), `connection.test.ts` (backoff and the resume probe, driven through the public `subscribe` path), `frozen-recovery.test.ts` (a REAL server and a REAL socket: the reaped socket is abandoned, re-dialed and resubscribed with no event of any kind — A/B'd, it times out with the watchdog disabled) and `native/resume.test.ts` (the property name both shells hardcode, and inertness when nothing is subscribed).
- **A run whose terminal frames died with the socket is recovered from the TRANSCRIPT, not from a reload.** A backgrounded tab (or a frozen phone) loses the channel, the turn finishes with nobody reading, and `message_end`/`agent_end` go into a socket that no longer exists — so the timeline keeps the stream's last PARTIAL segment and its local generating flag never releases, while the SERVER's `stream` row settles normally (which is why the docked indicator stops but the answer stays cut off mid-sentence). `client/hooks/chat/timeline/lost-run.ts` (`useLostRunRecovery` → `useReconnectSettle` in `client/hooks/chat/omp/reconnect.ts`) repairs it on the channel's own reopen: settle the local run (release the flag, clear the placeholder/optimistic/pending-steer ids, and drop `thinking.isGenerating` so no accordion pulses over a finished answer), then re-read `/api/chat/:id` — unconditionally, because the rows on screen are the stream's snapshot and the JSONL is the only record of the rest, so `session-load.ts`'s own guard (which keeps a LAGGING read away from a live run) does not apply. **Three conditions decide whether it acts, and each was a way to destroy a healthy turn.** (1) The socket must have been OPEN and then gone: a dial is not a reconnect, so a send whose `connect()` opens a closed channel is excluded — that is the window where the optimistic turn exists but the prompt may not be on the wire. (2) There must be evidence a run was IN FLIGHT, and it is TWO independent facts (`streamRunRef || serverRunRef`): the stream announced it (set by every non-owned `setGenerating(true)` — `agent_start` and the mount probe's resume — never by the send path's owned arm), or the server wrote its `stream` row at dispatch. The second is not redundant — a run whose very FIRST frame (`agent_start`) was the one lost has no stream-side mark on this client, and keying on `streamRunRef` alone left that case unrecoverable (the timeline kept an empty placeholder while the answer streamed into a socket that was gone). A prompt that has not reached the server has no row, so the dispatch window stays excluded either way. (3) The server must say the run is over: `runIsOver` (`client/hooks/chat/omp/run-liveness.ts`) accepts `running:false`, rejects `busy` (a live subagent, a parked dialog), a live `isStreaming`/`isPromptRunning`, a non-zero `queuedMessageCount` (the session WILL wake again), and requires omp's own verdict to be literally `isSettled === true` — the same rule the server's `RunSettle` applies (`isSettled !== true` → do not settle), because a turn can be admitted but not yet streaming and two answers to "is this run over" is how the indicator and the transcript end up disagreeing; a build that does not report the field therefore settles nothing, and an unanswered probe is no verdict at all — settling a live run throws away the streaming answer, while missing a settle costs one more reconnect. An empty transcript read leaves the rows alone AND is retried on `SESSION_META_RETRY_SCHEDULE_MS`, for the same reason `refreshSessionMeta` retries: omp buffers its transcript writes, so the turn that just finished can be missing from the file for a beat after the channel returns — one read and stop would leave the partial answer in place, which is the state this recovery exists to clear. The retry is bounded and stops at the first non-empty answer, and the placeholder ROW is deliberately not deleted before the replacement arrives (only its mark is released), because dropping the only row first would blank the transcript for the retry window and for good if omp never flushed. Pinned by `lost-run.test.ts` (mounted through the real `useSessionLoad`: the partial row is replaced by the committed turn and the flag releases; a live run, a first dial and an unanswered probe all leave it untouched), `reconnect.test.ts` (the trigger conditions and the once-only settle) and `run-liveness.test.ts` (the verdict table); `tests/e2e/specs/reconnect.e2e.ts` is the assembled proof of the OUTCOME (a real child streams, the channel is held unreachable past the end of the run, and the full answer appears with no reload, one row, nothing spinning) — but it is NOT the discriminator for the mechanism: verified by disabling `useLostRunRecovery`, the spec still passes, because the session-load effect re-running as the URL settles repairs the transcript in that shape too. The mechanism is pinned at the unit layer and each branch was A/B'd there. `FAKE_OMP_PERSIST=1` makes the scripted child write its turn to the session JSONL the way omp does; without it there is nothing to read back and the spec would pass for the wrong reason. **Three failures shaped the trigger, and all three are now pinned by tests.** (1) A run whose very FIRST frame was the one lost had no stream-side mark, so the trigger now accepts the SERVER's `stream` row as independent evidence (`streamRunRef || serverRunRef`). (2) The client's generating flag FLICKERS false during a live run (a `prompt_result` folded as settled, the spawn-adoption window), so a drop landing in one of those beats was read as "nothing was running" — the evidence is now LATCHED (`facts.sawRun`), and a flicker cannot erase it. (3) The hook re-mounts while the CHANNEL does not — the effect is keyed on the session id, which moves mid-run when a spawn answers with omp's UUID — so local variables came back with `wasOpen:false` and the following drop read as a dial that never opened; the facts live in a ref outside the effect and `wasOpen` is reconciled from the client's own status on every render. `wasOpen` is read from `realtimeClient.status()`, never `socket.readyState`: the browser flips readyState to OPEN BEFORE dispatching `open`, and a spec gating on it fired inside the `connecting` window (measured).
- **Session auto-titling is ONE-SHOT per conversation, retried ONCE after the first run, and it runs on the SESSION's model** (`src/server/lib/omp/session/auto-title.server.ts`, fired from the `message_end` and `agent_end` branches of `src/server/lib/omp/rpc/frame-fold.ts`; the request lifecycle is `session/title-request.server.ts`). omp force-sets `PI_NO_TITLE=1` under `--mode rpc-ui`, so Kabmo asks for the title itself by sending `/rename` to the child. **The trigger is the first settled USER message, not `agent_start`** — omp pushes `agent_start` before the turn opens, so the transcript is still empty there and a `/rename` at that point reads `messageCount: 0` and is answered "Could not generate a session title" (measured on omp 18.3.0: the user message lands ~120ms after `agent_start`). `message_start` is equally too early — omp appends the message to the agent's state on `message_end`, so only that frame makes `get_state` report it. `agent_end` then retries ONCE as the fallback, for the early attempt that came back empty (provider error, timeout, child gone, or the tiny model declining); it stands down when the name already landed. Never fire it beyond that: omp derives the title from the NEWEST turns and `generateRenameTitle` reserves a fresh title revision (cancelling any generation still in flight), so an attempt after turn two names the session after whatever the conversation had become by then. The eligibility latch is `AgentSessionWrapper.autoTitlePending`, seeded in `applyIdentity` from omp's own `messageCount` (a `--resume` child reports the messages it restored, so an existing session is ineligible from the start) and consumed by the settle attempt — the early attempt deliberately does NOT consume it, or a first-turn failure would leave the session unnamed for good. `autoTitleRequested` (reset at each `agent_start`) keeps a queued steer message from re-asking inside the same run. **The fallback ALWAYS dispatches, and the two dispatches are chained.** It used to stand down while the early attempt's claim window was still armed, on the assumption that generation would land — measured on a real session it had already failed 52 s earlier, so the run ended with nothing asked again and the session kept `New Session - <timestamp>` forever. `sendTitleRequest` queues behind the dispatch in flight because omp cancels a superseded rename WITHOUT emitting the frame that would release its claim. **And the request runs on the conversation's own model, which is what made it work at all.** omp names a session with its `tiny` ROLE and only appends the session's model when `retry.modelFallback` is on; measured here, six auto-title attempts across four fresh sessions all ran on `kenari/deepseek-v4-flash` (plan lapsed → `429 plan_limit_reached` at ~52 s) and named nothing, while the two renames that succeeded — the manual action — ran on `kenari-jatis/deepseek-v4-1-flash` through `withTitleModelOverride`. That pin is now written for BOTH paths and held until the request's frames arrive (omp resolves the role again for the card request after a successful title, so releasing on the ack produces an uncarded title); the overlay is newest-wins rather than queued, because a title model can take a minute and a second request must not wait on the first. **Claims are COUNTED, not one slot** (`autoTitleClaims`): with a single slot the second request overwrote the first, so the first one's `command_output` reached the timeline as a notice row. **A prompt sent after a run end waits for the dispatch** (`awaitTitleDispatch` in `session-commands.ts`'s `prompt` branch): both are separate frames on one stdin, and a prompt admitted first lets the rename name a conversation that has already moved on. The wait is the ack (~3 ms), bounded at `TITLE_DISPATCH_BARRIER_MS`, never the generation. **A landed title republishes the sidebar** (`session_info_update` in the fold calls `publishSidebarStructure`): the title is rewritten in a fixed-width slot inside the session file, so the mtime the scan cache keys on does not move — measured, the list kept the placeholder while the header already showed the name, until an unrelated tool call republished. A first message omp's low-signal filter rejects ("hi") therefore leaves the session unnamed for good — the honest outcome, since the fallback does re-ask and omp declines a second time (the rejection is a property of the opening message, not of when it is asked). **omp 18.9.0 added title CARDS and Kabmo carries them through rather than stripping them**: `title.icons` (default `nf+emoji`) heads a generated or renamed title with an icon and a short code — `formatCardTitle` emits `🧪 FLAKY: Fix flaky park tests` — and `sessionTitleFor` returns `session.name` verbatim, so the sidebar renders exactly what omp's own TUI renders. No card-aware branch is needed (the prefix is part of the name omp persisted), and a user who wants plain names sets `title.icons: boring`, a row the panel gained with the 18.9.2 schema. The card is also why `/rename` now takes longer: a title with no card triggers a SECOND title-model request for the card (`renameTitle` → `#cardIcons` → `generateTitle(named, cardPrompt)`), which the 60 s `TITLE_CLAIM_WINDOW_MS` has room for.
- **A `prompt_result` that opened no turn is suppressed for as long as the operator's turn streams, and the RUN is the attribution — not the `/rename` request window.** `frame-fold.ts` forwards that frame only when the run is not streaming; the auto-title window (`autoTitleWindowUntil`) was not enough on its own, because it is armed around the `/rename` and omp answers that command within the same tick (measured ~3 ms on omp 18.7.0), so an ack that won the race reached the client anyway. The client folds this frame as "the run settled" (`agent-events.ts` → `onPromptSettled`), which blanks the docked indicator and drops the optimistic user mark — so the turn's own echo was appended beside the bubble it stood for, and Kabmo's copy of the conversation held `msg-…-user` and the echoed row side by side for one prompt (measured on real sessions; the indicator died ~450 ms into a 20 s run, and the turn rendered twice). The rule covers every auxiliary sender that answers on this frame — a Plan/Goal toggle, `/reload-plugins`, `rename-with-ai`, a peer instance's command — and the run's flags are left alone, exactly as the dispatcher leaves a non-owning command's (`rpc/session-commands.ts`). A builtin the operator typed while the agent is IDLE still passes through untouched: that is the case the frame exists for.

### Internal-URL Reads (`read xd://…`, `skill://`, `omp://`)

- **`read xd://<device>` is the device's DOCUMENTATION, not a call, and it is the READ panel.** omp's `XdProtocolHandler#usage` answers a read of `xd://` (the listing), `xd://<device>` and `xd://<device>/<topic>` with `xdevDocs` — the `# <name> — <title>` page — and its own reader has no `xd://` read card (unlike `proc://` and `cfg://`), so it draws an ordinary read. Dispatching by device instead sent the doc page to the device's panel: `read xd://lsp` drew the LSP diagnostics card, `read xd://mcp__codegraph_explore` the MCP argument/result card, and `read xd://eval/browser` (the `eval` device's `browser` topic) the eval card — three panels for one class of call. `isXdDocRead`/`xdDocPath` (`tool-renderers/registry.ts`) gate on the **READ transport plus an `xd://` path**, tested before `xdDevice`; the WRITE transport is untouched, because `write xd://lsp` genuinely IS a call. `xdDocPath` returns the FULL path — `xdDevice` reports `eval` for `xd://eval/browser` and the card named the wrong thing.
- **A doc read's URL is its identity, so the card shows it.** `toolCardSubtitle` hides a bare `xd://` URL because for a device CALL the URL is transport (the device names the action); for a doc READ the URL is what was read. The card was otherwise `Read Done` with `aria-label="Expand Read"` — nothing naming it. Verified in the browser: `Read · xd://eval/browser · 77 lines`.
- **A path with no language answers `text`, not `javascript`.** `getLanguageFromPath` defaulted to `javascript` for an extensionless file, an internal URL or a web URL, so a `read` of one was badged `JAVASCRIPT` and its prose was tokenized as code. omp's own `getLanguageFromPath` returns `undefined` for the same input, and `text` is a Shiki SPECIAL language — always loaded, no grammar fetched, emitted escaped and uncoloured — so the badge and the tokenizer both stop claiming a language the input does not have (verified: `src/x.ts` still reads `typescript`). A `read xd://<device>` reached this badge only until it was given its own markdown branch (below); it now reads `Markdown`.
- **The read SELECTOR comes off the path first, or the language is lost with it.** `read` may name its lines on the path itself — `src/App.tsx:23-300:raw` — and the extension was read off the raw string (`tsx:23-300:raw`, an unknown extension), so `getLanguageFromPath` answered `text` and the code surface rendered unhighlighted. Only a `:N-M` suffix was ever stripped; measured on a real session store, **9517 of 18128** read paths carry a selector and **1268 of those — every `:raw`, `:img` and comma list — were missed**. `stripReadSelector` (`shared/lib/fs/paths.ts`) peels omp's whole grammar (`:N`, `:N-M`, `:N-`, `:N+K`, `:-N`, a comma list, `:raw`/`:conflicts`/`:img`, and a compound `:1-50:raw` / `:raw:-60`), mirroring `splitPathAndSel` in `@oh-my-pi/pi-tui/tools/read.ts` — including its trailing rule (a chunk must END in a digit, `.` or `-`, which is what rejects the in-flight `:50+`) and its conservatism about a bare `a:1-50` filename. A URL is returned untouched: its colon can be a PORT (`http://host:8080/x` would otherwise lose `:8080` to the line-range rule) and no fs route ever receives one. It is used by the language detector, the Read panel's `/api/fs/read` + `/api/fs/raw` path (`x.png:raw` read as an unknown extension, so the image sniff said "not an image") and `getFileExtension`. Verified in the browser on the `:23-300:raw` sample: badge `tsx`, 41 Shiki spans coloured; the plain `app/types/chat.ts` read still reads `TYPESCRIPT` with 63 coloured spans.
- **A `text/markdown` read renders as prose.** omp draws it as the document it is (`details.contentType === 'text/markdown' ? md(text) : code`); the panel had no markdown branch, so `read skill://diagnose` rendered `# Diagnose ## Steps 1. Reproduce` as one code block — the file's headings, lists and links came out as literal syntax. 86 internal reads carry that content type (`skill://` 11, `omp://` 22+, `agent://`), and 38 real `.md` file reads do too. The source is omp's own `displayContent.text`, never `rawContent`: omp prefixes every row of the raw output with its file line number (`1|# Diagnose`) and a markdown parser reads that gutter as content, turning the heading into the paragraph `1|# Diagnose`. The gutter-stripping parser (`parsedCode.cleanCode`) is only the fallback, because it needs TWO numbered rows to recognise the format — a one-row document (`read xd://propose`, a real payload) kept its `1|` prefix through it and rendered as the paragraph `1|Write your plan's <slug> …`.
- **A `read xd://<device>` is markdown too, even though omp tags it `text/plain`.** The device documentation (`# <name> — <label>`, `## Schema`, a ```ts fence) is the same prose a `skill://`/`omp://` read carries; only `XdProtocolHandler`'s hardcoded `contentType: "text/plain"` distinguishes it, and the panel had no branch for it — measured over every `xd://` read of a real install (220 rows, 11 distinct documents): all of them rendered as one `<pre>` of literal syntax behind a `TEXT` badge, `# ast_edit — AST Edit` and `## Schema` included. `isXdDocRead` widens the markdown predicate, so the badge reads `Markdown` and the prose renders as prose (verified in the browser: badge `Markdown`, no `<pre>`, the card body is `.prose-content`). A FAILED doc read is excluded — `read xd://nope` answers "No such tool: …", a refusal rather than a document, and it keeps the code surface.
- **The demo dataset carries a doc read.** `virtual-devices-session` renders `read xd://eval/browser` with the measured `details` (`contentType: text/plain`, `totalLines`, `displayContent`), so the badge, the URL subtitle and the prose surface are all exercised under `MOCK=true`. `tools-session` likewise carries `call_01b_read_selector` — a read whose path is `src/App.tsx:23-300:raw` — so the selector strip is exercised in the demo, not only in a test.

### Image Reads (tool renderers)

- **A `read` of a picture answers with the picture, so the panel paints the RESULT's bytes — never a re-read of the path it names.** omp's toolResult carries `{type:'image', data, mimeType}` beside its text note, and externalizes anything large to the content-addressed blob store (`blob:sha256:<hash>`; measured on this install, 421 of 435 image blocks across the session files are refs). Both parse paths now carry it: `extractToolImages` (`src/shared/lib/omp/session/tool-images.ts`) is applied by the reload pass (`collectToolOutputs` + the inline `toolResult` blocks) and by the live fold (`recordToolResult`, `tool_execution_end`), landing on `ToolCallData.images`.
- **The old path-only reconstruction was broken for exactly the reads people screenshot.** The panel pointed `/api/fs/raw?path=<target>` at the tool call's path, but that route only serves paths inside the browse scope and `read` routinely names one outside it (`/tmp/shiki96.png`) — so the panel drew a broken image while the bytes sat in the transcript. `readFailed` (`status: 'error'` / `isError`) suppresses the fallback too: a failed read ("Path '/tmp/x.png' not found") names a picture that is not there, and pointing an `<img>` at it drew a broken thumbnail over an error message.
- **Blob bytes are served by `GET /api/omp/blob?hash=` (`src/server/routes/omp/blob.ts`).** `readBlobImage` resolves the content type from the BYTES first (`getImageMimeFromBytes`, magic-byte table in `file-kind.ts`) and from the sibling extension link second, because omp stores the blob under its content hash and the extension lives only on a hardlink beside it — and because the type is not cosmetic: a 96×96 PNG read came back as a JPEG blob, and `image/*` is what makes the browser paint instead of download. The hash feeds a `path.join` against the store, so `readBlob`'s canonical-digest guard is the security boundary (verified: `../../etc/passwd` and a truncated digest both 404 before touching the filesystem). An inline payload becomes a `data:` URL instead — re-encoding megabytes into every session load is what the store exists to avoid.

### Injected Entries and Narration (what omp writes for the model)

- **Every injected `custom_message` renders, including the ones omp itself hides.** omp writes prompt injections with `display:false` and its own transcript walk skips them; Kabmo deliberately does not, because a session's JSONL is the record a reader may want in full. So `mid-run-todo-nudge` ("7 todo items still open…" — **468 entries** across the author's 931 session files), `todo-error-reminder`, `plan-mode-context` (6.8 KB), `plan-mode-reference` (27.5 KB), `image-attachment-description`, `orchestrate-notice` and `eval-state-context` all appear as "System Notice" cards.
