The slider (`setInitialScale`, re-applied on every `onResume`) is the page's STARTING scale and does NOT block a pinch: measured on Android 15 with a real two-finger gesture (a `sendevent` sequence on `/dev/input/event1`, since `adb input` cannot do multitouch), the viewport went 1 → 2.883 with `setInitialScale(100)` in force, and a cold start returned it to 1. **Two traps cost the most time here and both are about the test, not the code**: the emulator still zoomed with `setSupportZoom(false)` compiled in (verified with `dexdump`), so the setting is not the only path into the gesture there and an A/B on it proves nothing; and every early "pinch does nothing" measurement was taken against a page that had not loaded (the server was down, so the WebView showed `chrome-error://`), where no gesture zooms. Reproduce against a loaded page before concluding anything.
- **The shell draws a launch overlay from the tap until the console paints, and it is a SIBLING of the WebView — never a view inside the page.** The page cannot report its own readiness (its bundle is what renders the app), and a page-drawn overlay would vanish exactly when the page fails to load. `LoadingOverlay` (platform views, like the settings screen) is added to a `FrameLayout` above the WebView; `LoadingState` holds the rules and is JVM-tested, because every one of them is an ordering the platform actually produces and each fails as a stuck or missing overlay rather than as an exception. **The bar is indeterminate until real progress arrives**: a determinate bar at 0 is indistinguishable from a stuck one, and the first seconds of a cold start are when the user decides whether the app is broken — so Chromium's 0% tick is ignored. **100% is not a finish** (`onPageFinished` is, and the shell is still fetching the bundle at 100%), and progress never moves backwards within a load (a redirect reports 100 then 10, which made the bar snap back and read as a stall). **A failed main-frame load keeps the overlay up with a reason and Retry / Server settings** — host, connect, timeout, SSL, catch-all — and `onReceivedError` is gated on `request.isForMainFrame`, because a page that loads but cannot reach one of its assets reports an error too and treating that as a failed page would cover a working console. **`onPageFinished` never clears a failure**: a failed load still finishes on the error page Chromium renders, and hiding the overlay there is exactly the blank paper screen with no explanation the overlay exists to remove; Retry reloads the saved address because that error page is not a document the console can recover from. **`startLoad` (cold start, saved-URL change, Retry) and a RELOAD re-open the overlay** — a plain in-app navigation does not, or the login submit would flash it like a reload. The page's own Reload control is a different thing and was the bug: `location.reload()` fetches the SAME address again, which from the shell's side is a cold start in miniature (the console is replaced by a blank document while the bundle is re-fetched), and the overlay stayed hidden so the header's button looked like it did nothing. **The only signal a reload gives is that the url is the one already on screen** — measured on Android 15: `onPageStarted` reports no reload flag, `doUpdateVisitedHistory` says `isReload=false`, and a `location.href = '/other'` navigation reports a different url, while `location.reload()` and `location.replace(location.href)` both report the same one. `LoadingState.isDocumentReload(startedUrl, shownUrl, phase)` is that test, and it requires phase `DONE` because `startLoad` arms the overlay itself before `loadUrl`, so the first `onPageStarted` of a cold start would otherwise restart its own arm; the shown url is LATCHED in `onPageFinished`, never read from `view.url`, which already points at the incoming document by the time the next load starts. Verified end to end against a real console behind a 20s-delayed bundle: clicking the header's Reload put the wordmark and `Loading the console… 51%` on screen, and the same click without the delay completed too fast to see. Verified on a real emulator against a 4s-stalling stub and a dead port: the wordmark + `Loading the console… 10%` mid-load, no progress node once painted, and `Could not connect…` + Retry over the dead port with a tap recovering to the live page. **The wordmark is the ARTWORK README.md embeds, not the app's name in a `TextView`**: `res/drawable/kabmo_wordmark.xml` is a port of `public/kabmo-wordmark.svg` (same plate, same three K strokes, same outlined word, same colours) because the overlay is platform views and has no SVG renderer, and the three differences are forced by the format — every y shifted `+128.12` (the SVG is authored around a baseline at 0 and a viewport cannot start below the origin), the plate a path rather than a `<rect>`, and the gradient inline in viewport space. A copy of one drawing is exactly what drifts silently (the overlay still renders, just not the mark), so `WordmarkDrawableTest` re-derives the drawable from the SVG — every path with only y shifted, the plate against the rect, the gradient span and both stops, the stroke weight and per-stroke colours — and fails on a nudged hex. `app_name` stays as the `ImageView`'s `contentDescription`.
- **ALL FIVE shells draw one launch overlay, and the wordmark in it is GENERATED from the README artwork.** Android and iOS had it from the start (a sibling of the web view, four phases, a wordmark, a bar, Retry, a failure that `didFinish` cannot clear); the three desktop shells each drew a `Kabmo` TEXT FIELD and a spinner instead, which is a different product for the same few seconds. They now share the contract: `SplashState` (`packages/macos/Sources/KabmoDesktop/SplashState.swift`, `packages/windows/src/KabmoDesktop/SplashState.cs`), `LoadingState` (Android, iOS) and `Progress` (`packages/linux/src/splash.rs`, drawn by `splash_view.rs`) are the same four phases (`connecting`/`loading`/`done`/`failed`), the same three progress rules (a frame after the end is ignored, progress never moves backwards, a zero frame stays indeterminate), the same five failure reasons with one sentence each, and the same reload test. **The artwork is the reason this is one bullet rather than five**: `public/kabmo-wordmark.svg` is the ONE copy, `scripts/shell-wordmark.mjs` GENERATES `Wordmark.swift`, `Wordmark.cs` and `wordmark_data.rs` from it, and each shell's rules suite re-derives every number from that same file (the iOS `WordmarkTests`, `packages/macos/Tests/SplashTests.swift`, `packages/windows/tests/Program.cs`, `packages/linux/rules/wordmark.rs`) — a hand-typed copy is the shape that drifts silently, because the splash still renders, just not the mark. `bun run scripts/shell-wordmark.mjs --check` is a CI gate in `gates.yml`. **A platform that cannot do what the others do says so rather than imitating it**: WebView2 exposes NO progress estimate (`NavigationStarting`/`ContentLoading`/`DOMContentLoaded`/`NavigationCompleted` and nothing between), so the Windows bar sweeps for the whole load instead of inventing a percentage, while WebKit and WebKitGTK give one through KVO/`estimated-load-progress`; `UIProgressView` has no indeterminate state, so iOS slides a `SweepBar` segment along the same 180pt track (two bars sharing one `barRow`, or the hidden one collapses and the sentence jumps every phase change); and **the reload signal differs per platform** — WebView2 reports `CoreWebView2NavigationKind.Reload` explicitly, while `WKNavigationDelegate` and WebKitGTK's `load_changed` have no flag at all, so macOS, iOS and Linux compare the started URL against the one LATCHED when a document finished (`isDocumentReload`, and only from `done`, or a cold start would restart its own arm). **The macOS splash was in the app target and could not be tested, which is how it shipped as a text field**: `SplashView` moved into `KabmoDesktop` so `TestsWindow` renders it in a real window and asserts the assembled view — the mark present and in the artwork's aspect, the bar determinate only once a real percentage arrives, the sentence the state's, Retry only on a failure — because a splash that drew the wrong phase passed every state-machine case.
- **The Android shell has exactly ONE settings entry point: the launcher shortcut.** Long-press the Kabmo icon → *Server settings*. There is deliberately no in-app control — no edge tab, no floating button, no Settings → Device row — because the WebView fills the display and every edge belongs to Kabmo's own chrome (a floating button sits on a control it is not; an edge tab covers the transcript's scroll gutter). The shortcut intent targets `MainActivity`, never `SettingsActivity`: a shortcut is launched with the LAUNCHER's identity, so a non-exported target is refused with `Permission Denial: … not exported from uid` (measured on a real APK — the shortcut silently did nothing). `SettingsActivity` therefore stays unexported and `MainActivity` opens it, handling the action in BOTH `onCreate` and `onNewIntent` because `singleTask` delivers a re-launch to the running instance. That is also why `window.KabmoNative` and its `DeviceSettings` category were removed rather than left as a second door: a control that opens a screen the shell already owns is a second thing to keep in sync, and the shell-side entry is the only one that survives a saved address that no longer answers.
- **The WINDOWS shell cannot resize at all, and `WindowChrome` is why the code looks like it can.** `WindowChrome { CaptionHeight = 0, ResizeBorderThickness = 6 }` answers `WM_NCHITTEST` for the PARENT window, but the WebView2 is a child HWND covering the whole client area, so the mouse never reaches the parent's hit test and the page gets the `mousedown` instead. Every edge and corner is therefore un-resizable and the 6px band is a reservation nothing can grab — the same shape `MicrosoftEdge/WebView2Feedback#4538` reports (a full-window WebView2 under `WindowChrome` cannot be resized; a `Margin` around the control is the only thing that makes the border live again), closed as a tracked feature request and folded into `#2243`, which asks for `app-region` to grow a resize meaning and is still open. `#2243` also confirms `app-region: drag` is the ONLY non-client behaviour WebView2 exposes, which is why the drag works here and the resize does not. The fix is Gantry's `ResizeFrame`: the PAGE renders eight thin edge/corner strips with the right cursors and calls a bridge command, and the shell posts `WM_NCLBUTTONDOWN` with the matching `HT*` code after `ReleaseCapture` so Windows runs its own interactive resize. NOT done — it is a shared-client change (a strip per edge on every layout) and this shell cannot be RUN on a macOS host, only compiled. `packages/windows/README.md` states the defect rather than claiming the border works; do not restore that claim without the strips.
- **The macOS shell is a native Swift package, and its window chrome is the one part a webview host cannot inherit.** `packages/macos` (Swift 6 + AppKit + WKWebView, no Xcode project, no third-party deps) is split the way `packages/ios` is: `Sources/KabmoDesktop` holds every rule that can be tested without a screen (`Persisted`, `Store`, `System`, `ShellWindow`'s pure `draggedOrigin`), and `Sources/kabmo-desktop` is the AppKit app. The SwiftPM **product** is named `Kabmo`, not `kabmo-desktop`, because a process with no bundle reports its executable's file name — `swift run kabmo-desktop` put `kabmo-desktop` in the menu bar (measured with `NSRunningApplication`), and `scripts/build-app.sh` copies the product to `Contents/MacOS/Kabmo` so the plist, the binary and the menu bar agree. The data directory is `~/Library/Application Support/Kabmo`, with `migrateLegacyStateDir()` moving `kabmo-desktop`'s state on the first launch after the rename (only when the new directory is absent).
  **The drag hook lives in `sendEvent`, not `mouseDown`** — measured: a press inside a `WKWebView` never reaches `NSWindow.mouseDown`, so a `mouseDown` override is dead code that looks correct. `sendEvent` asks the page whether the press landed on `[data-drag-region]` through `dragRegionSelector`, and the follow is manual arithmetic (`beginDrag` → `draggedOrigin(windowOrigin:startMouse:pointer:)`, a pure function) rather than `performWindowDragWithEvent:`: the native loop reads the physical pointer through an event tap and cannot be driven or tested (`AXIsProcessTrusted: false` on a normal machine — measured before, during and after, all identical), while the manual form is asserted by `TestsWindow` against a real `ShellWindow`. Double-click obeys `AppleActionOnDoubleClick` (`DoubleClickAction.current()`), the bar carries the system's own right-click menu (`barMenu()`), and `validateUserInterfaceItem` claims only close/minimise/zoom/full-screen so an unrelated action cannot be swallowed.
  **The frame's RESIZE BAND is claimed before the page too, and that is why diagonal resizing did nothing.** `fullSizeContentView` puts the page under the bar, so the page marks its whole top 40px `[data-drag-region]` — including the 3px strip along the top edge and the band around both top corners that `NSThemeFrame` reserves for dragging the window's SIZE. Measured through the real `ShellWindow`: a press at `(1, H-1)`, `(6, H-6)`, `(W-1, H-1)` and `(W/2, H-1)` all answered "the page says drag handle" and started a window MOVE, so every diagonal resize (both top corners, and the top edge itself) moved the window instead of resizing it. The bottom, left and right edges kept working because the page marks nothing there — which is exactly the reported shape, "diagonal resize does nothing" while the plain edges are fine. `frameOwnsPress(at:)` is the predicate: `contentView.superview.hitTest` at the press point, and the answer is the FRAME's when the hit is neither the content view nor inside it. It is deliberately not a hard-coded band thickness — measured on macOS 27 the band is 3px along the top edge, 4px along the bottom and the sides, and **8px** into the top-left corner because the close button's own margin is reserved there, so a fixed probe point passes on one macOS and lands in the page on the next. `sendEvent` breaks out of the claim for a frame press (so `super.sendEvent` starts AppKit's own resize loop), the right-click menu refuses the band the same way, and `claimsBarPress` carries both so the window suite asserts the decision rather than restating its inputs. `runResizeBandCases` measures where the band ENDS and asserts the header is a drag handle again past it, in both directions — a fix that simply refused every press in the top strip would pass a one-sided case. Pinned by `TestsWindow` against a real `ShellWindow`; the fix was A/B'd, with the three "not claimed as a window drag" cases failing when `frameOwnsPress` was removed.
  **The traffic lights are claimed by the SYSTEM before the page is asked, and that ordering was the bug that made close / minimise / zoom dead.** The page marks its whole header `[data-drag-region]` and reserves its leftmost 76px for the buttons (`trafficLightInset`), so `elementFromPoint` at a traffic light answers "a drag handle" — the window claimed the press, `super.sendEvent` never ran, and the button never saw the click, while the same strip still moved the window. `sendEvent` now tests `hitsStandardButton(at:)` FIRST and breaks out of the claim (so the press passes through to the button). That predicate is `standardWindowButton(_:)` for `.closeButton` / `.miniaturizeButton` / `.zoomButton`, converted to window coordinates — the only way to reach them, since they live in the private titlebar container and not in `contentView`, and their frames are AppKit's to decide. A hidden button is skipped so a window without `.closable` reserves no dead strip. `TestsWindow` pins both directions: a press inside each button's own frame is not a drag and reaches the button, and the gap just past `trafficLightInset` is still a drag (the fix must not turn the reserved strip into dead window). Its own harness had to move: the "blank header" case pressed at x=60, which is INSIDE the close button's frame, so it was asserting the bug as correct behaviour — the page-owned point is `trafficLightInset + 24`.
  **The Edit menu is what gives the page its clipboard chords, and without it the composer ignores ⌘V.** A `WKWebView` receives NO ⌘C/⌘V/⌘X/⌘Z/⌘A unless the app's menu bar declares those key equivalents — the web view is the first responder and does see the `keydown` (a page reads `metaKey` and `key === 'v'`), but the edit COMMANDS travel through the menu system, so with no item claiming the chord AppKit never asks the responder chain, `paste:` never runs and the page's own `paste` event never fires either. The shell built App, File, View and Window and nothing else, which is why the macOS app's composer silently ignored the keyboard's clipboard chords. Measured A/B on a bare `WKWebView` against the same window with the menu: ⌘V left the field unchanged with no `paste` event versus `paste-proof`; ⌘A left the selection `4-4` versus `0-11`; ⌘C left the pasteboard `nil` versus the selected text; ⌘X left `alpha` in the field versus an emptied field with the text on the pasteboard; ⌘Z/⌘⇧Z did nothing versus `hi` → empty → `hi`. `EditMenu.swift` holds the row table (`editMenuItems`) and the gate (`editMenuHandles`), and four details are load-bearing because each fails silently: **`undo:`/`redo:` have no AppKit-declared selector** (they are `NSResponder`'s informal protocol, absent from every public header) so they are spelled as strings while `NSText`'s `cut:`/`copy:`/`paste:`/`delete:`/`selectAll:` are used as selectors so a typo is a compile error rather than a dead row; **`Redo` carries `⌘⇧Z` EXPLICITLY**, because a menu item's default mask is `.command` and `keyEquivalent: "z"` alone collides with Undo and makes the row unreachable; **`ShellWindow.validateUserInterfaceItem` must allow the rows** — it answered `false` for every action it did not name itself, and `undo:`/`redo:` resolve against the WINDOW (measured: `NSApp.target(forAction:)` answers `NSWindow` for those two and `WKWebView` for the other five) whose own default answer is `false`, so the menu was built, the chords were declared, and ⌘Z still did nothing; and **the chords need a KEY window**, so a suite driving them must `NSApp.activate` first or the key-equivalent pass never runs. `TestsWindow/main.swift` drives all of it into a real `WKWebView`, and its undo case runs FIRST because WebKit keeps ONE undo stack per document — run after the cut case, ⌘Z undid the cut and the field came back as `cut-me` (measured), which reads as "undo is broken" when the ordering was what was wrong. With the menu removed, 8 of those checks fail.
  **Quit was slow for three independent reasons, and each was measured rather than guessed.** (1) `applicationWillTerminate` ran a `Task` and then `DispatchSemaphore.wait`ed on the MAIN thread — and `NSApplicationDelegate` methods are `@MainActor`, so the task that would signal needed the very actor blocked in the wait: the app beachballed for the full 6 s timeout on EVERY quit. It is now `applicationShouldTerminate` + `.terminateLater`, AppKit's own deferral, with the work detached and `reply(toApplicationShouldTerminate:)` ending the quit. (2) The spawned child inherited a BLOCKED SIGTERM: `posix_spawn` copies the caller's signal MASK, and `POSIX_SPAWN_SETSIGDEF` resets only the DISPOSITION — the app blocks SIGTERM, so `kill(-group, SIGTERM)` left it pending, the child never died, and `killGroup` always ran its full 40 × 50 ms budget. Verified A/B: with only `SETSIGDEF` the child "died from SIGTERM? false"; adding `POSIX_SPAWN_SETSIGMASK` with an empty set, "true". **2.27 s → 0.058 s per server.** (3) A zombie answered "alive": `isAlive` polled `kill(pid, 0)`, which still answers 0 for an exited-but-unreaped child, so the grace loop never saw it go. It now uses `waitpid(WNOHANG)` (which also reaps) and falls back to the signal probe on `ECHILD` for an adopted pid. The escalation is also COLLECTED now, not just sent — returning straight after `SIGKILL` left the child a zombie for the next caller. `Tests/main.swift` measures all three (an exited child is not alive; a well-behaved child is released in under 0.5 s; a SIGTERM-ignoring child is still killed) and each case fails with its fix reverted.
  **Linux had the same two shutdown faults, and one more.** `is_alive` polled `kill(pid, 0)` (same zombie bug, same fix — `waitpid(WNOHANG)`); and `stop_all()` ran on the GTK main loop, freezing the window for the whole grace budget, so it now runs on its own thread with `MainContext::invoke` posting the quit back (the `Supervisor` moved from `Rc` to `Arc` for that). It does NOT have the mask bug: Rust's `Command` resets the signal mask for the child itself. Windows was already correct on both counts — its `Closed` handler is on the UI thread but every await in the library is `ConfigureAwait(false)`, so the continuation cannot queue behind the wait.
  **Both test suites are plain executables that exit non-zero**, because `XCTest` ships with Xcode and this package builds with the Command Line Tools alone: `swift run kabmo-desktop-tests` (170 checks, no window) and `swift run kabmo-desktop-window-tests` (a real window and webview).
  **Settings › asks the PAGE for its modal, and a reload is not that.** Every settings screen is a client-rendered modal, so the shell has nothing native to show — the row's job is to dispatch the one event both layouts already listen for, `omp:open-settings` (`PageEvents.swift`, `openSettings(in:)`). What it used to do was append `?settings=1` and `load` the URL again, and **nothing on the client reads that parameter**: the modal never opened while the live document was thrown away — a running turn, the composer draft and the timeline's scroll position with it. The Linux shell had already sent the event, so the contract was known and this shell simply was not using it. The case in the window suite dispatches the SHIPPED script into a real page and asserts the listener fired once, the detail is empty, and the document SURVIVED (`documentElement.dataset.probe` kept), which is the half that fails if the row ever reloads again — verified by restoring the reload, which fails 4 cases including the later typing ones because the page was replaced. `release/macos.test.js` pins the two silent halves: the row goes through the helper rather than a query parameter, and the name the Swift constant declares is the name `settings/Modal.tsx` listens for (a typo either side is a dispatch into the void).
- **The macOS shell is released by a version BUMP, not a publish — and `packages/macos/version.properties` is the ONE place its version is declared.** `release/macos.js` holds the table (`MACOS_SHELL`), `readVersionName`, `replaceVersionLine` and the watermark; `release/release-macos.mjs` bumps it from the same Conventional Commits rules the app uses; `release/macos-version.mjs` prints it for `scripts/build-app.sh`, which fills `CFBundleShortVersionString` and `CFBundleVersion`. `.github/workflows/publish-macos.yml` is a two-phase reusable body: `build` compiles the package on BOTH architectures (`arm64` on `macos-15`, `x64` on `macos-15-intel`), runs both Swift suites, verifies the version the plist declares, the ad-hoc signature and the binary's own `lipo -archs` slice, and uploads one `Kabmo-<version>-mac-<arch>.zip` per row — and `release.yml` calls it as `macos-build` BEFORE `app` tags, so a Swift error or a plist that disagrees with the properties file stops the release instead of shipping a tag whose release carries no app. `macos-app` attaches that artifact after the release exists and builds nothing. `app` `needs: macos-build`, so the build is a gate rather than a consequence.
  **The archive is made with `ditto`, never `zip`.** `zip -r` strips the bundle's extended attributes and code signature, and macOS then refuses to launch the extracted app ("damaged") with nothing in the archive to say why; verified by extracting the published shape and re-running `codesign --verify --deep --strict`.
  **There is deliberately no User-Agent token, unlike the Android and iOS shells — but the console DOES offer this app an update.** The version comes over the BRIDGE instead: the shell injects `__kabmoDesktop` before the page's first script, and `desktopShellFromIdentity` reads `shellVersion` from it, so the sidebar's offer is decided on the first paint. A UA token would mean every asset request carried a string the bridge already supplies. **The reported version must come from the BUNDLE** (`ShellVersion.current()` → `CFBundleShortVersionString`, which `scripts/build-app.sh` fills from `version.properties`): `App.swift` used to declare `shellVersion: "0.1.0"` as a literal, so the pipeline bumped the properties file to `0.2.0` while the running app went on telling the console `0.1.0` — a comparison against a version that did not exist. `release/macos.test.js` asserts both halves (no `KabmoMacOS` token, and no literal version in `App.swift`).
- **`tests/ci-gates.test.ts` is the contract, asserted on the YAML graph rather than on file text.** Every job in a releasing workflow must reach both gate jobs TRANSITIVELY (so a publish job added without `needs` fails the test rather than shipping), every local `uses:` must resolve to a file that declares `workflow_call`, every `with:` key must be declared by the callee, no gate job may carry an `if:`, the chain `packages → marketplace → android → ios → windows → macos` must hold (the lockfile and bump ordering, which the transitive check cannot see), the shell bump must run and commit BEFORE `apk-build` compiles and BEFORE `app` tags, the APK must be built before the tag and only attached after it, and `publish.yml`'s gate jobs must pin the `ref` they check. Two traps are pinned by its own implementation: `needs` is a STRING for one dependency and an array for several (spreading the string yields its characters), and the array must be COPIED before `stack.pop()` — handing out the parsed array drains the job's own `needs` as the walk consumes it, so the second job through the closure reports a gate it does reach as missing (measured).

---

### GitHub Bot & Review Automation

`@kabmo-labs-bot` is a GitHub App plus the workflows in `.github/workflows/`; it triages every new issue and reviews every pull request. `CONTRIBUTING.md` is the contributor-facing half of this contract — read it before changing any of these files, because the two must tell the same story.

- **Writes go through the App, never `GITHUB_TOKEN`.** Every bot job mints an installation token from `BOT_APP_ID` + `BOT_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` (`actions/create-github-app-token`), so comments and labels come from `@kabmo-labs-bot` and cannot re-trigger a workflow that matches bot comments. Jobs are skipped outside `kabmo-labs/kabmo`: a fork run has no App secret.
- **Command surface**, first line of a comment, exact match: `@<BOT_MENTION> help|review|summarize|triage|reproduce [focus]` (plus the `/oc-review` alias). `help` is answered without a model — it is a fixed string. The mention comes from the repository variable `BOT_MENTION` (default `kabmo-bot`) rather than a literal, so an App whose slug differs still triggers: `bot-config-check.yml` is the one place that reads the App's real slug from its own installation token and reports the value to set.
- **The agent is the pinned `omp` CLI, run headless.** `.github/scripts/install-omp.sh` owns the version pin (`OMP_VERSION`); `.github/scripts/run-omp-bot.sh` runs `omp -p` with a persona file, a policy overlay, and the prompt on stdin. The runner needs `oven-sh/setup-bun`; `omp` is a Bun program.
- **Two provider modes, picked by whether `BOT_API_BASEURL` is set.** Unset: `BOT_MODEL` is a provider omp already knows and `BOT_API_KEY` is exported as the env var that provider reads (`BOT_API_KEY_ENV`, e.g. `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`). Set: the model lives behind an OpenAI-compatible gateway, so the script declares a provider for the run from `.github/bot/provider-models.yml` and passes the key with `--api-key`. The second mode exists because omp ships no provider for an arbitrary base URL, and because **`--api-key` overrides the file's value while a `models.yml` `apiKey` cannot reference an environment variable** (`$VAR` and `${VAR}` are both literally true strings — verified against a local OpenAI-compatible stub) — so the template carries a placeholder and the real secret never touches the runner's disk. A thinking level (`BOT_THINKING`) only reaches the provider when the model entry declares `reasoning: true` — verified against the stub: without that line `--thinking` is accepted and silently dropped, with it a `--thinking high` run arrives as `reasoning_effort: "high"`. The template carries the line; a model's declared `efforts` may never include `off` (the schema rejects it, and omp clamps `off` up to the lowest listed effort). Repository settings: secret `BOT_API_KEY`; variables `BOT_MODEL`, `BOT_API_BASEURL` (optional), `BOT_API_KEY_ENV` (required only when `BOT_API_BASEURL` is unset), `BOT_THINKING` (optional, default `high`), `BOT_MENTION` (optional, default `kabmo-bot`). The three provider settings are read from either store (`vars.X || secrets.X`), so a value placed in Secrets still works. **`BOT_MENTION` must be a variable**: every command trigger is a job condition, and a job condition may read `github`, `needs`, `vars` and `inputs` — never `secrets` — so a secret of that name is silently ignored and the default is used instead (`bot-config-check.yml` reports exactly that).
- **Policy is data, in the `.github/bot/*.yml` policy overlays.** `tools.approval.<tool>: deny` plus a `bash.patterns` allowlist ending in `- match: "*"` / `deny` gives a deny-by-default session; the reviewer cannot edit a file or run anything but `gh`, `git`, `rg`, `ls`, `cat`. **Never write `approval: prompt`**: there is no UI on a runner, so a prompt is a rejected call that reads to the model as a broken tool.
- **Procedure is versioned with the repository**, in `.omp/skills/{pr-review,issue-intake}/SKILL.md`, read by the agent through `skill://`. The persona files (`.github/bot/*.persona.md`) hold identity, guardrails and the exact output contract; the skills hold the steps. A 25 KB prompt never lives in a workflow.
- **Nothing downstream trusts the model.** `verify-review-verdict.sh` re-reads the posted comment from the API, requires the final line to be the marker `<!-- omc-review-meta {"head":"…","verdict":"…"} -->`, checks that the marker's HEAD equals the requested HEAD and is still the current one, that the human-readable verdict and reviewed-HEAD lines agree, and only then writes one `review:*` label. A mismatch fails the job and marks `review:automation-failed`; the verdict itself never fails a check. The human-readable checks read **values, not decoration** (backticks, bold, whitespace): a live run posted a correct review with every backtick stripped on the way to the API, and a gate stricter than the generator turns a finished review into a wasted run — the HEAD must still be named as a complete token, so tolerance applies to formatting and never to content.
- **Readiness is a label, not a comment**: `review:pending|ready|needs-evidence|blocked|human-required|automation-failed`. Exactly one at a time — `set-review-status.sh` is the only writer. Drafts carry none.
- **Trust boundary**: `pull_request_target` runs with secrets, so the base is what is checked out and the head is fetched as passive git data — never installed, built, or executed. A diff touching `AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `.github/{workflows,scripts,bot,ISSUE_TEMPLATE}/**`, `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` or `.omp/skills/**` is refused (`review:human-required`): automation must not clear a change to its own policy.
- **Logic lives in `.github/scripts/*.sh`, not in YAML.** Each script takes arguments and reads its state through `gh`, so it can be exercised locally against a stubbed `gh`; the workflow steps stay thin glue. When adding a gate, add it as a script.
- **Never put user content into a `run:` block through `${{ }}`** — title, body, comment text and focus go through `env:` and are read as shell variables. `run-omp-bot.sh` pipes the assembled prompt to `omp` on stdin for the same reason: no interpolation into shell text, no argument-quoting hazard.
- Editing any of these files is a `ci`-type change; the review-policy paths above mean the pull request that changes them will be marked `review:human-required` and must be read by a human.

---

## Codebase Architecture & File Organization Rules

### 1. File Size Ceiling (Hard Limit: 350 Lines)

- **Maximum 350 Lines Per File**: Every TypeScript and TSX file must strictly stay under 350 lines of code (`wc -l` result `< 350`).
- **Decompose before you hit the limit**: When a file approaches the ceiling, split it immediately — never let it cross 350 and never "temporarily" exceed it.
- **Extraction patterns** (pick the one that matches the code):
  - Large render blocks ➔ sub-components in the parent's kebab-case folder (`git-panel/TreeView.tsx`).
  - Stateful logic clusters ➔ custom hooks under `src/client/hooks/<domain>/`.
  - Pure functions / constants / validators ➔ `src/shared/lib/<domain>/` modules.
  - Large callback objects / factories ➔ factory functions taking a single `deps` record (preserves closure semantics exactly).
  - Shared low-level utilities ➔ a sibling `shared/` or `utils.ts` inside the feature folder.
- **Verify**: `find src -name "*.ts" -o -name "*.tsx" | xargs wc -l | grep -v total | awk '$1>350'` must print nothing.

### 2. Folder & Filename Conventions (Clean Names)
- **Symmetrical folders**: A component's folder mirrors its name in **kebab-case**, and the folder's main entry is `index.tsx`:
  - `ChatTimeline` ➔ `src/client/components/workspace/chat-timeline/index.tsx`
  - `GitPanel` ➔ `src/client/components/workspace/git-panel/index.tsx`
  - `SessionSidebar` ➔ `src/client/components/layout/session-sidebar/index.tsx`
  - `ModelDropdown` ➔ `src/client/components/workspace/model-dropdown/index.tsx`
- **Suffix-only filenames**: When the folder already supplies the context, child files DROP the redundant prefix and keep only the suffix:
  - `src/client/components/workspace/model-dropdown/Header.tsx` — not `ModelDropdownHeader.tsx`
  - `src/client/components/workspace/git-panel/TreeView.tsx` — not `GitTreeView.tsx`
  - `src/client/components/workspace/chat-timeline/tool-renderers/panels/Bash.tsx` — not `BashPanel.tsx`
  - `src/client/hooks/chat/timeline/actions.ts` — not `useChatTimelineActions.ts`
  - `src/shared/lib/omp/rpc/manager.ts` — not `rpc-manager.ts`
- **No duplicate basenames in the same folder**: if two files would collide, move one to its correct domain folder or give it a distinguishing suffix (e.g. `panels/SearchTool.tsx` vs `panels/SearchFs.tsx`).
- **`index` is reserved for the folder's real entry point** (the main component/hook implementation) — never a re-export barrel (see rule 3).
- **Naming case**: kebab-case for folders and multi-word non-component files (`active-project.ts`, `notification-sound.ts`); PascalCase for Preact component files; `useXxx` prefix only when the file is a standalone reusable hook at a domain root.
- Reusable or cross-cutting components belong in `src/client/components/common/`.
- No empty, abandoned, or ghost folders (`src/applet/`, duplicate domain folders, etc.).

### 3. Direct, Explicit Imports (No Barrel Clutter)
- Do NOT create re-export-only `index.ts` barrels inside `src/client/components/`, `src/client/hooks/`, or `src/shared/lib/` — they pollute editor fuzzy-search and hide file origins.
- `index.tsx`/`index.ts` is allowed ONLY when it contains the folder's actual implementation (the main component/hook), not a list of `export ... from` statements.
- Import the concrete module explicitly via the `@/` alias:
  - `import { ChatTimeline } from '@/client/components/workspace/chat-timeline';` (folder entry)
  - `import { Header } from '@/client/components/workspace/model-dropdown/Header';`
  - `import { useChatTimeline } from '@/client/hooks/chat/timeline';`
  - `import { toTitleCase } from '@/shared/lib/chat/title-case';`
- The only intentional barrel is `src/shared/types/index.ts` (the central type barrel).

### 4. Absolute Imports via `@/` Alias (No Relative Imports)
- **All** internal imports MUST use the `@/` path alias (mapped to `./src/*` in `tsconfig.json`, which Bun's bundler reads directly). Relative imports (`./`, `../`) are **forbidden** in application code.
- This applies to every import form: `import`, `import type`, `export ... from`, and side-effect imports.
- Examples:
  - `import { ChatTimeline } from '@/client/components/workspace/chat-timeline';`
  - `import type { OmpSession } from '@/shared/types/omp/session';`
  - `import { getDb } from '@/server/db.server';`
  - `import '@/client/tailwind.css';`
- Exceptions (keep relative):
  - Third-party packages and node built-ins (never prefixed with `@/`).
  - Assets outside `src/` (e.g., `package.json` at the project root) — use a relative path.
- The `~` alias is deprecated; use `@/` exclusively.
- **Verify**: `grep -rnE "from '\.\.?/" src --include='*.ts' --include='*.tsx' --include='*.js' | grep -v node_modules` must print nothing. `src/cli/**` is covered by this gate too — it is Bun-run plain ESM, so `@/cli/...` resolves there like everywhere else.

### 4b. Import Preact Directly (zero React packages)
- **Never write `from 'react'` or `from 'react-dom'` in `src/`.** Import the runtime directly:
  - Hooks → `import { useState, useEffect, useRef } from 'preact/hooks';`
  - Components, context, portals, and React-shaped types → `import { memo, Suspense, lazy, createContext, createPortal } from 'preact/compat';`
  - Generic element/event types → `import type { TargetedMouseEvent, TargetedKeyboardEvent } from 'preact';`
- **Zero-react state:** package.json contains no React packages and `tsconfig.json` carries no `react*` alias. The three former shim consumers were replaced: `react-icons` → `lucide-preact` + static brand SVGs (`src/client/components/common/file-icon/`), `react-simple-code-editor` → CodeMirror 6 in `src/client/components/common/code-surface/` (see the editor bullet above), `react-resizable-panels` → the hand-rolled group/panel/separator trio in `src/client/components/layout/desktop-layout/resizer/`.
- **Adding dependencies:** if a package imports `'react'` in its published code, either pick a Preact-native alternative or hand-port the small surface you need — never re-introduce a react→preact alias to accommodate it.
- **Event types:** Preact's `MouseEvent`/`KeyboardEvent` from `preact/compat` are generics requiring one type argument. Use `TargetedMouseEvent<HTMLElement>` for JSX handlers, and the DOM's own `globalThis.MouseEvent`/`globalThis.KeyboardEvent` for native `addEventListener` callbacks and xterm handlers.

### 5. Server vs Shared vs Client Modules
- **Three-way split.** `src/server/**` is Bun-only (see the Runtime section at the top), `src/client/**` is browser-only, and `src/shared/**` is imported by both. Never import a `node:` builtin or `bun:sqlite` from `src/client/**` or `src/shared/lib/**`; if a shared module needs one, it belongs in `src/server/lib/**` instead.
- Non-UI modules MUST be grouped into domain subfolders — never dumped flat in the root of `src/client/hooks/`, `src/client/data/`, `src/server/lib/`, `src/shared/lib/`, or `src/shared/types/`.
- **`src/client/hooks/<domain>/`**: `chat/`, `ui/`, `workspace/`, `browser/`, `models/`, `settings/`. Split further when a family grows: `chat/timeline/` (timeline state + actions), `chat/omp/` (live agent bridge), `settings/providers/` (provider settings state, API calls, actions, messages).
- **`src/shared/lib/<domain>/`**: `chat/`, `code/`, `fs/`, `markdown/`, `models/`, `omp/`, `workspace/`. `omp/` splits into `core/`, `rpc/`, `session/`, `config/`; `models/` splits into `provider/` for the provider family (dialect, presets, models, connection, cleanup, label, glyph).
- **`src/server/lib/<domain>/`**: the same domain tree, holding only the modules that touch Node/Bun builtins or the database. A server family splits the same way (`omp/session/usage/` holds the aggregator and its presentation shaping).
- **`src/client/data/<domain>/`**: `settings/`, `samples/`, `mock/`, `models/`, `theme/`, `agent-data/`, `context-data/`.
- **`src/shared/types/<domain>/`**: group related interfaces (`settings/`, `omp/`); truly cross-cutting types stay at the root.
- Grouping changes are atomic: move the files AND update every importer in the same change.

### 6. Pure UI Components & Semantic Separation
- **`src/client/components/` is for Preact UI (`.tsx`)**. Co-located pure helpers are allowed when scoped to that feature, but must be `.ts` and live under the feature folder or its `shared/` (e.g. `tool-renderers/shared/detect-format.ts`, `editor/utils.ts`).
- Anything reusable beyond a single feature belongs in `src/shared/lib/`, never in a component folder.
- **Dedicated non-UI directories**:
  - **Hooks (`src/client/hooks/<domain>/`)**: Custom Preact hooks.
  - **Data (`src/client/data/<domain>/`)**: Mock or static datasets.
  - **Types (`src/shared/types/<domain>/`)**: Domain interfaces and types.
  - **CLI (`src/cli/`)**: The `kabmo` command-line entry (`kabmo.js`) and its `lib/` (arg parsing, process lifecycle, registry, serve/update/stop/restart/status/logs). These stay `.js` (plain ESM, no build step) and run under Bun via the `#!/usr/bin/env bun` shebang. `pkgRoot` is resolved two levels up from `src/cli/kabmo.js`; keep that depth if the file ever moves.

### 7. Domain Types Architecture
- Domain data models and shared TypeScript interfaces must be organized cleanly under `src/shared/types/`:
  - `workspace.ts` — folders, session entities, and sorting types
  - `fs.ts` — file explorer node trees, opened files, and search result items
  - `git.ts` — git changes, branch lists, and view mode states
  - `chat.ts` — messages, agent actions, monologue, and attachments
  - `settings/` — settings state and per-category settings shapes (`agent.ts`, `command.ts`, `mcp.ts`, `project.ts`, `provider.ts`, `skill.ts`, `state.ts`)
  - `omp/` — oh-my-pi bridge types (`session.ts`, `agent.ts`)
  - `index.ts` — central export barrel for all types (`import type { ... } from '@/types'`)

### 8. Verification Requirements
- Every change must pass:
  1. `bun run lint` (`tsc --noEmit`) without errors.
  2. `bunx tsc --noEmit --noUnusedLocals --noUnusedParameters` without errors.
  3. `find src -name "*.ts" -o -name "*.tsx" | xargs wc -l | grep -v total | awk '$1>350'` no file over 350 line codes
  4. Production build verification (`bun run build`).
- **Unused Code Check (MANDATORY before task completion)**: Before declaring any task done, verify no unused imports, locals, or dead props were introduced or left behind:
  - Fix every `TS6133` (declared but never read), `TS6192` (all imports unused), `TS6196` (declared but never used), and `TS6198` (all destructured elements unused) error.
  - Remove unused imports (icons, types, components) and unused destructured props/state — do not leave dead code behind.
  - If a component's props/state become unused because a feature was stubbed or removed, strip them from the interface, the destructure, and every call-site in the same change.
  - Do NOT ship `import React from 'react'` in `.tsx` files — the Preact JSX transform makes it unnecessary (keep named imports like `useState`).
- **Structural Checks (MANDATORY for refactors that move/rename files)**:
  - No file exceeds 350 lines (rule 1 command).
  - No relative imports remain (rule 4 command).
  - No duplicate basenames inside the same folder.
  - Every moved/renamed file's importers are updated in the same change; grep for the old path returns nothing.

### 9. Layout & Panel Resizing
- **Two width units, deliberately.** The **sidebar** is stored in **pixels** — it holds a session list, which does not get more useful on a wider monitor. The **editor and the right panel** are stored as a **share of the group's available area** (a fraction), because a pixel width is only right on the display it was dragged on. A slot may carry both; the fraction wins when the area is known, and the px value is what it renders at before then.
- **Sizes are px at the resizer boundary.** The hand-rolled resizer (`src/client/components/layout/desktop-layout/resizer/`) is px-only end to end: fixed panels get a px flex-basis and one filler panel absorbs the remainder. A fraction is resolved to px *before* it reaches a `Panel` — never pass a percentage string into `defaultSize`/`minSize`, because `toPx` parses it with `parseFloat` and `"45%"` would silently become 45px.
- **The panel ceiling is dynamic, not a constant, and the editor + right panel are a COUPLED PAIR.** `resolvePanelWidth` (`src/shared/lib/workspace/panel-widths.ts`) caps a panel by the smaller of three things: its own max (`PAIR_MAX_FRACTION` = 0.6), the pair total minus what the sibling reserves (`PAIR_MAX_TOTAL_FRACTION` = 0.7), and the group budget (`available − chat floor − the sibling's reserved width − handles`). The defaults are editor 0.4 + right 0.3 = 0.7, so growing one forces the other down and neither can reach 0.6 while the other is open. The right panel has ONE default fraction for every view (`DEFAULT_RIGHT_PANEL_FRACTION`) — a per-view table cannot coexist with the pair, because the two defaults must sum to the total (a 0.6 terminal beside a 0.4 editor is 1.0); views differ only by their floor and their remembered width. `WorkspacePanels` resolves the RIGHT panel first (reserving the editor's WANTED share), then the editor (reserving the right panel's ACTUAL width) — that asymmetry is what makes an editor asking past its default push the right panel down first, with the pair still summing to 0.7. Reserving only a sibling's FLOOR is the shape that broke: the two declared widths summed past the group, flexbox shrank BOTH, and the right panel rendered 15px under its share (measured at 1440 with both open: editor asked 507 / rendered 477, right asked 245 / rendered 230). Verified in the browser at 2K: defaults render editor 40% / right 30%; an editor wanting 0.5 renders 50% / right 20%; an editor wanting 0.9 caps at 60%. The pair cap is computed in PIXELS and ROUNDED (`0.7 × 4000` is `2799.9999…` in binary floating point).
- **One remembered width PER PANEL, never per group**: the sidebar, the editor panel (a separate width for source tabs and for diff tabs), and each of the eleven right-panel views (`context`, `files`, `search`, `git`, `wiki`, `terminal`, `user-browser`, `browser`, `usage`, `todo`, `plan`) each own their width — the sidebar's lives in its own module (see the GLOBAL rule below), the rest in `src/shared/lib/workspace/panel-widths.ts` (data) and `src/client/hooks/workspace/panel-widths.ts` (state); the single default fraction, px defaults, per-view minimums, and the view list live in `src/shared/lib/workspace/right-panels.ts`. Sharing one number between panels — or resetting a panel to a hard-coded width when it is toggled or switched — is exactly the bug this shape exists to prevent.
- **The sidebar's width is GLOBAL, and that is the one exception to "per session".** It lives in `app_settings.omp_sidebar_width` (`src/shared/lib/workspace/sidebar-width.ts` + `src/client/hooks/workspace/sidebar-width.ts`), beside the sidebar's other cross-session state (`omp_sidebar_expanded_sessions`, `omp_sidebar_sort`) — never in `layout.panelWidths`. The sidebar lists the same sessions in every chat and only the highlight moves, so a width that changed when the user picked a different session read as the layout jumping on its own. It was not always global: `0004c95` (2026-09-23) swept the sidebar into the per-session map along with the editor and the right panel, which had a real reason to be there, and `9d361a9` (2026-10-08) then dropped the `desktopLayoutSizes` seed that had been keeping a dragged width global — so a `left` slot existed in `layout.panelWidths` for two weeks and 120 sessions on the author's install carry the same seeded 351 while a handful carry a local drag. `resolveSidebarWidth` therefore migrates by PRECEDENCE — stored setting, else the retired GLOBAL `desktopLayoutSizes.left`, else the open session's slot — because adopting a session slot first would move the sidebar 351 → 500 on the first switch. A `left` slot is deleted from a session blob on the first open after the upgrade and dropped by `mergePanelWidths`, so no stored state keeps a second answer.
- **Widths are per session for the editor and the right panel, and a new session starts EMPTY.** They live in `session_ui_state` under `layout.panelWidths`, beside the rest of a session's layout state (`layout.activeRightPanel`, `layout.showRightPanel`, `layout.showLeftPanel`, `layout.userToggledEditor`, `layout.openedFiles`), so switching sessions restores the layout that session was left in. A session that has never been resized has no `panelWidths` at all, so every panel resolves from its own default fraction (`DEFAULT_EDITOR_FRACTIONS`, `DEFAULT_RIGHT_PANEL_FRACTION`) or px fallback. **There is deliberately no global seed for these** — a former `app_settings.desktopLayoutSizes` carried the last global drag into every new session, and since the per-session migration nothing wrote it, it froze at a stale value and pinned a new session's editor to its 320px floor (measured) while the diff tab opened normally. Writing to it could not repair it either: the write would have to run before the session's own blob loads and would clobber it with the global value. The SIDEBAR is the exception and is global — see the rule above.
- **A pending `new-…` session's UI state is loaded like any other session's, because it is persisted like any other.** The id is what the URL carries (`?sessionId=new-…`), so a reload of a pending chat — a dev-HMR reload, or a second tab — re-enters that slot with a row already on the server. `SessionStateProvider` used to short-circuit it as "fresh, nothing stored yet" and mark it ready without a fetch; every consumer then showed its fallback (the repo picker reset to the workspace root, the right panel jumped back to `files`) **and the next write destroyed the stored blob**, because `persistSession` posts the WHOLE in-memory map — a state that was never loaded persisted as one key. `markSessionReady` existed only for that short-circuit and is gone; the only special case left is spawn adoption (`migrateSessionState`), which reads the transient slot's now-hydrated cache.
- **A private `useSessionState` copy re-reads when the store REPLACES a session's blob — and only then.** Every caller holds its own `useState`, so two components on one key never see each other's writes; that is deliberate (a panel width belongs to one component). But `ready` does not move when a spawn adoption migrates the pending slot onto the omp UUID, so the copy kept the fallback it read on the commit the URL changed and every right-panel view the user had opened collapsed at the first send of a new chat (measured through a real provider mount: `true:git` → `true:files`). `subscribeSessionReplaced` (`shared/lib/workspace/session-state/listeners.ts`) is a SECOND bus, separate from the per-slot one, fired by the wholesale paths only — `loadSession`, `hydrateSession`, `migrateSessionState` (both ids) and `forgetSession`. A per-slot write deliberately does not fire it: announcing one would make every editor and panel copy in the app re-read on every keystroke elsewhere.
- **A resize drag is deferred.** The separator moves a ghost guide line and re-applies the real width at most every `RESIZE_FOLLOW_INTERVAL_MS`; the panel's own 200ms width transition smooths the steps. Applying the width on every `pointermove` reflows an xterm buffer, a CodeMirror document and a browser iframe per frame, for a width the user is still choosing.
- **A collapsed panel stays mounted.** `Panel`'s `collapsed` prop renders it at zero width and marks it `data-panel-collapsed`, so its state (tree expansion, terminal buffer) survives and the width transition has something to animate. The separator walks past collapsed panels when it resolves its neighbours — pairing a drag against a zero-width panel whose registry floor still reads 320 clamps every delta to zero and kills the handle.
- **Restore through props, not imperative rebuilds**: every panel's `defaultSize` is derived from the remembered width for its current slot, and `Panel` adopts a changed `defaultSize` itself — that covers a right-panel view switch and an editor source↔diff switch, both of which keep the panel mounted while only its width slot changes. No layout cache exists to go stale. Append an entry here if a new resizable group is introduced.

### 10. Route Organization & Domain Grouping
- **Domain-Based Subdirectories**: Routes under `src/server/routes/` MUST be organized and grouped into subdirectories matching their functional domain (e.g., `src/server/routes/settings/`, `src/server/routes/chat/`, `src/server/routes/fs/`, `src/server/routes/terminal/`, `src/server/routes/telemetry/`, `src/server/routes/sessions/`, `src/server/routes/files/`, `src/server/routes/folders/`).
- **No Monolithic Flat Folder Clutter**: Do NOT dump API endpoints loosely in the root of `src/server/routes/` as flat files. `src/server/routes/index.ts` is the only file that enumerates domains; every domain folder owns its own modules and never imports a sibling domain.
- **One Elysia plugin per domain**: Each domain folder exports `HandlerBinding[]` built from its route modules (see `src/server/lib/route-adapter.ts`). Route modules keep the ported Remix shape — an exported `loader` (GET) and/or `action` (mutating verbs) — so status codes and payloads stay identical to the pre-migration API. Prefixes are declared once, in `routes/index.ts`.

### 11. Environment Data Modes (`MOCK=true` vs `MOCK=false`)
- **`MOCK=true` (Simulation & Demo Mode)**:
  - All features and loaders utilize rich predefined datasets and presets from `src/client/data/` (simulated demo chats, token telemetry ranges, agent/project presets).
  - Database seeding automatically injects sample workspace folders, demo commit sessions, and file structures.
- **`MOCK=false` (Real Data Mode)**:
  - The application operates strictly against real backend resources and real SQLite database persistence.
  - No synthetic sample sessions, fake dialogues, or hardcoded mock files are auto-injected.
  - Managed globally through `Bun.env.MOCK` and verified via `@/server/mock.server`.
